FlashAttention-2 works on Ampere so the attention path is fine. |
| `mlsys23-validating-large-language-models-with-re` | Validating Large Language Models with ReLM | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/93c7d9da61ccb2a60ac047e92787c3ef-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/93c7d9da61ccb2a60ac047e92787c3ef-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mkuchnik/relm) | System that compiles regular expressions into constrained decoding over an LLM - single-GPU inference on GPT-2 or GPT-J class models and a direct ancestor of todays structured-generation stacks - concern - the largest models in the paper need quantization to fit 24 GB |
| `mlsys24-awq-activation-aware-weight-quantization` | AWQ: Activation-aware Weight Quantization for On-Device LLM Compression and Acceleration | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/42a452cbafa9dd64e9ba4aa95cc1ef21-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/42a452cbafa9dd64e9ba4aa95cc1ef21-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mit-han-lab/llm-awq) | Weight-only 4-bit quantization plus the TinyChat W4A16 inference engine - a user-space, single-GPU artifact. Concern - the 70B-on-mobile headline is out of reach; scale-down to 7B/13B on the A5000 is the realistic target. Widely used code, low build risk. |
| `mlsys24-atom-low-bit-quantization-for-efficient` | Atom: Low-Bit Quantization for Efficient and Accurate LLM Serving | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/5edb57c05c81d04beb716ef1d542fe9e-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/5edb57c05c81d04beb716ef1d542fe9e-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/efeslab/Atom) | W4A4 mixed-precision quantization with custom kernels integrated into a serving path; single-GPU throughput study. Concern - INT4 mma tensor-core paths are supported on sm_86 but the kernels were tuned on A100/RTX4090, so kernel performance on the A5000 needs re-tuning; no code URL in the PDF but the official repo is labelled "[MLSys'24] Atom" by the authors' lab. |
| `mlsys24-keyformer-kv-cache-reduction-through-key` | Keyformer: KV Cache reduction through key tokens selection for Efficient Generative Inference | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/48fecef47b19fe501d27d338b6d52582-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/48fecef47b19fe501d27d338b6d52582-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/d-matrix-ai/keyformer-llm) | KV-cache eviction policy (key-token selection) for decode-phase memory/bandwidth - exactly the llm-inference topic and single-GPU by nature. Concern - accuracy evaluation on long-context summarization benchmarks costs GPU hours; 7B-class models only. |
| `mlsys24-prompt-cache-modular-attention-reuse-for` | Prompt Cache: Modular Attention Reuse for Low-Latency Inference | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/a66caa1703fe34705a4368c3014c1966-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/a66caa1703fe34705a4368c3014c1966-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/yale-sys/prompt-cache) | Precomputed, reusable attention states for repeated prompt segments - a caching/TTFT problem evaluated on single GPU and even CPU. Small, readable prototype; obvious improvement axes (cache admission/eviction, positional handling). Concern - the schema-based reuse can perturb output quality, so an improvement claim needs an accuracy control. |
| `mlsys24-punica-multi-tenant-lora-serving` | Punica: Multi-Tenant LoRA Serving | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/054de805fcceb78a201f5e9d53c85908-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/054de805fcceb78a201f5e9d53c85908-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/punica-ai/punica) | Multi-tenant LoRA serving; the core artifact is the SGMV CUDA kernel plus a batching/scheduling layer, both of which work on one GPU. Concern - the paper's scheduler evaluation uses a small GPU cluster; on a single 24 GB A5000 the scale-down is a 7B base model with many adapters. Kernels are sm_80+ Ampere-friendly. |
| `mlsys24-q-hitter-a-better-token-oracle-for-effic` | Q-Hitter: A Better Token Oracle for Efficient LLM Inference via Sparse-Quantized KV Cache | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/bbb7506579431a85861a05fff048d3e1-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/bbb7506579431a85861a05fff048d3e1-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/VITA-Group/Q-Hitter) | Joint KV-cache sparsification + quantization (quantization-friendly heavy hitters); repo link is in the paper. Single-GPU, 7B-class models. Concern - much of the released code is accuracy-side; end-to-end throughput/memory gains may need to be wired into a serving engine by the team. |
| `mlsys24-qmoe-sub-1-bit-compression-of-trillion-p` | QMoE: Sub-1-Bit Compression of Trillion Parameter Models | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/c74b624843218d9b6713fcf299d6d5e4-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/c74b624843218d9b6713fcf299d6d5e4-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/IST-DASLab/qmoe) | Sub-1-bit MoE compression format with bespoke GPU decoding kernels for compressed inference - the decode kernel and format work on one GPU. Concern - the SwitchTransformer-c2048 headline needs 3.2 TB of weights and a 160 GB output, which does not fit the ~257 GB free disk or the download budget; only the smaller Switch base/large-128 models are realistic, so the project must be framed around the kernel/format at reduced scale. |
| `mlsys24-slora-scalable-serving-of-thousands-of-l` | SLoRA: Scalable Serving of Thousands of LoRA Adapters | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/906419cd502575b617cc489a1a696a67-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/906419cd502575b617cc489a1a696a67-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/S-LoRA/S-LoRA) | Unified paged memory pool for adapter weights + KV cache, adapter prefetch from host memory, custom batched-LoRA kernels; the paper explicitly claims single-GPU operation. Strong fit for a memory-management or admission-policy improvement. Concern - tensor-parallel experiments are multi-GPU and must be dropped. |
| `mlsys24-sida-sparsity-inspired-data-aware-servin` | SiDA: Sparsity-Inspired Data-Aware Serving for Efficient and Scalable Large Mixture-of-Experts Models | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/698cfaf72a208aef2e78bcac55b74328-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/698cfaf72a208aef2e78bcac55b74328-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/timlee0212/SiDA-MoE) | MoE inference that offloads dormant experts to host DRAM and predicts expert activation - a GPU-memory/host-memory tiering problem that suits 24 GB GPU + 125 GiB RAM very well. Code URL given in the abstract. Concern - training the expert-activation predictor adds an offline cost; largest MoEs in the paper must be scaled down. |
| `mlsys24-vidur-a-large-scale-simulation-framework` | VIDUR: A LARGE-SCALE SIMULATION FRAMEWORK FOR LLM INFERENCE | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/b74a8de47d2b3c928360e0a011f48351-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/b74a8de47d2b3c928360e0a011f48351-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/vidur) | LLM-inference simulator + benchmark suite: schedulers, batching policies and parallelism can be studied without a cluster, which is ideal for a one-machine project. Released profiling data avoids needing the original hardware. Concern - validating the simulator against real runs on an A5000 requires re-profiling operators locally; multi-GPU configurations remain simulation-only. |
| `mlsys25-diffserve-efficiently-serving-text-to-im` | DiffServe: Efficiently Serving Text-to-Image Diffusion Models with Query-Aware Model Scaling | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/414fd191b3246a19a55741b938380136-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/414fd191b3246a19a55741b938380136-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/qizhengyang98/DiffServe) | Diffusion-model serving with query-aware cascades - cascade and scheduling logic is user-space Python and SD-class models fit in 24 GB - concern: paper evaluates a multi-GPU serving cluster so a single-GPU scale-down changes the resource-allocation experiments |
| `mlsys25-efficient-llm-inference-using-dynamic-in` | Efficient LLM Inference using Dynamic Input Pruning and Cache-Aware Masking | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/afd6374c7f2839cba22f537f15f4f760-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/afd6374c7f2839cba22f537f15f4f760-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Qualcomm-AI-research/dynamic-sparsity) | Dynamic input pruning plus cache-aware masking for memory-bound decoding - predictor-free, evaluated with simulated memory hierarchies so it runs on one GPU - concern: Phi-3-Medium at FP16 exceeds 24 GB, use the 7-8B configurations |
| `mlsys25-fasttree-optimizing-attention-kernel-and` | FastTree: Optimizing Attention Kernel and Runtime for Tree-Structured LLM Inference | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/96894468eb44631a32d7ebd56f9892c7-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/96894468eb44631a32d7ebd56f9892c7-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/PanZaifeng/FastTree-Artifact) | Radix-tree-aware attention kernels and runtime on top of SGLang - artifact states the Triton kernels use no Hopper-specific features, so an Ampere GPU works with reduced absolute numbers |
| `mlsys25-flashinfer-efficient-and-customizable-at` | FlashInfer: Efficient and Customizable Attention Engine for LLM Inference Serving | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/dbf02b21d77409a2db30e56866a8ab3a-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/dbf02b21d77409a2db30e56866a8ab3a-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer) | Customizable attention engine with block-sparse KV layouts and JIT templates - single GPU, Ampere supported, integrated into vLLM/SGLang so both baselines and improvement targets are easy - concern: some kernels are tuned for Hopper |
| `mlsys25-lserve-efficient-long-sequence-llm-servi` | LServe: Efficient Long-sequence LLM Serving with Unified Sparse Attention | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/cc8c6b9d89f7a898a29f58869b238e46-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/cc8c6b9d89f7a898a29f58869b238e46-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mit-han-lab/omniserve) | Unified static plus dynamic sparse attention for long-context serving - Llama-3-8B fits 24 GB and the kernels are Ampere-compatible INT/FP16 - concern: shared OmniServe repo with QServe, and the longest-context configs assume 40-80 GB GPUs |
| `mlsys25-leanattention-hardware-aware-scalable-at` | LeanAttention: Hardware-Aware Scalable Attention Mechanism for the Decode-Phase of Transformers | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/16ec6494e9b5a4138de7238761d715b4-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/16ec6494e9b5a4138de7238761d715b4-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime) | Exact decode-phase attention kernel (stream-K style splitting) - pure CUDA kernel work on one GPU - concerns: no standalone repo, the official implementation is the LeanAttention kernel merged into ONNX Runtime contrib_ops/cuda/bert, and paper numbers are on A100/H100 so absolute speedups on Ampere A5000 will differ |
| `mlsys25-milo-efficient-quantized-moe-inference-w` | MiLo: Efficient Quantized MoE Inference with Mixture of Low-Rank Compensators | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/9032e5c9ec394ce768a2fa9bdc56af6c-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/9032e5c9ec394ce768a2fa9bdc56af6c-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Supercomputing-System-AI-Lab/MiLo) | 3-bit MoE quantization with low-rank compensators and Tensor-Core kernels - Ampere supports the INT-based kernels - concern: Mixtral-8x7B at 3 bits is roughly 20 GB so it only just fits 24 GB and calibration-free quantization still needs large host RAM |

| `mlsys25-optimizing-llm-queries-in-relational-dat` | Optimizing LLM Queries in Relational Data Analytics Workloads | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/b5dc49f44db2fadc5c4d717c57f4a424-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/b5dc49f44db2fadc5c4d717c57f4a424-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/lynnliu030/artifact-eval) | Row and field reordering to maximize KV-cache reuse for batch LLM analytics queries - works against a single-GPU vLLM instance with Llama-3-8B and the win is measured as job completion time - reordering algorithm itself is CPU-side. Merge note: scout linked the subdirectory/branch https://github.com/lynnliu030/artifact-eval/tree/llmsql . |
| `mlsys25-qserve-w4a8kv4-quantization-and-system-c` | QServe:W4A8KV4 Quantization and System Co-design for Efficient LLM Serving | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/fbe2b2f74a2ece8070d8fb073717bda6-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/fbe2b2f74a2ece8070d8fb073717bda6-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mit-han-lab/omniserve) | W4A8KV4 quantization with co-designed GPU kernels - INT4/INT8 Tensor Cores exist on Ampere (no FP8 needed) and 7-8B models fit 24 GB - concern: paper headline results use A100/L40S, and the repo is shared with LServe |

| `mlsys25-xgrammar-flexible-and-efficient-structur` | XGrammar: Flexible and Efficient Structured Generation Engine for Large Language Models | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/5c20ca4b0b20b0bd2f1d839dc605e70f-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/5c20ca4b0b20b0bd2f1d839dc605e70f-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mlc-ai/xgrammar) | Constrained-decoding engine - grammar compilation is CPU-side and overlapped with GPU decode, runs with any single-GPU serving backend - widely deployed so baselines are easy to get |
| `mlsys26-accelerating-large-scale-reasoning-model` | Accelerating Large-Scale Reasoning Model Inference with Sparse Self-Speculative Decoding | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/66a026c0d17040889b50f0dfa650e5e0-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/66a026c0d17040889b50f0dfa650e5e0-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/sspec-project/SparseSpec) | Self-speculative decoding using a sparse-attention draft plus unified scheduling, delayed verification and dynamic KV management - all single-GPU systems mechanisms. Concern: headline numbers use 32B-class reasoning models that do not fit 24 GB in FP16, so scale-down or quantization is required |
| `mlsys26-blasst-dynamic-blocked-attention-sparsit` | BLASST: Dynamic BLocked Attention Sparsity via Softmax Thresholding | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/c6ee784cbe46d854843e4c883a3321ef-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/c6ee784cbe46d854843e4c883a3321ef-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/cameronshinn/blasst-ae-mlsys26) | Training-free blocked sparse attention driven by a single softmax threshold, with drop-in kernels for prefill and decode across MHA/GQA/MQA/MLA and a calibration procedure. Concerns: the artifact is delivered as a Docker or Singularity image and neither is installed, so dependencies (TensorRT-LLM, FlashInfer) must be rebuilt with conda; some kernels may assume Hopper features |
| `mlsys26-batchllm-optimizing-large-batched-llm-in` | BatchLLM: Optimizing Large Batched LLM Inference with Global Prefix Sharing and Throughput-oriented Token Batching | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/5b7ae1758452854dee4e962207d38304-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/5b7ae1758452854dee4e962207d38304-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/MixLLM) | Offline large-batch inference with global prefix identification and throughput-oriented token batching, released as a vLLM 0.6.4 fork. Throughput-oriented offline batching is one of the few serving regimes that a single 24 GB GPU can study honestly. Merge note: scout linked the branch https://github.com/microsoft/MixLLM/tree/batchllm_vllm_064 . |
| `mlsys26-breaking-the-ice-analyzing-cold-start-la` | Breaking the Ice: Analyzing Cold Start Latency in vLLM | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/29416b66c2149872b9d1415a3fd2c5e0-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/29416b66c2149872b9d1415a3fd2c5e0-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/upb-cn/vllm-startup-profiler) | Near-ideal fit: characterizes vLLM cold-start as a predominantly CPU-bound six-step process on one GPU, ships benchmark scripts and an analytical model. Improvement axis (cutting startup latency, better prediction) is directly testable on the A5000 |
| `mlsys26-cdlm-consistency-diffusion-language-mode` | CDLM: Consistency Diffusion Language Models for Faster Sampling | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/054de805fcceb78a201f5e9d53c85908-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/054de805fcceb78a201f5e9d53c85908-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SqueezeAILab/CDLM) | Diffusion-LM sampling acceleration with block-causal masks that restore KV-cache compatibility - pure inference-side systems angle. Concern: the consistency fine-tuning stage targets 7-8B diffusion LMs and likely needs more than one 24 GB GPU; released checkpoints make the inference/KV-cache half reproducible on the A5000 |
| `mlsys26-contextpilot-fast-long-context-inference` | ContextPilot: Fast Long-Context Inference via Context Reuse | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/b0131b6ee02a00b03fc3320176fec8f5-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/b0131b6ee02a00b03fc3320176fec8f5-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/EfficientContext/ContextPilot) | Context indexing, alignment and de-duplication to raise KV-cache reuse for long-context prefill, with a modular interface to existing engines. Prefill-bound, single-GPU workloads are well within the A5000's reach |
| `mlsys26-efficient-vram-constrained-xlm-inference` | Efficient, VRAM-Constrained xLM Inference on Clients | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/7cd265ae802235b8d5778a4a96ff22dd-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/7cd265ae802235b8d5778a4a96ff22dd-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/deepshnv/pipeshard-mlsys26-ae) | Best-matched paper on the list: pipelined sharding for CPU-GPU hybrid inference explicitly under a VRAM budget, built on llama.cpp, with an MLSys26 artifact repo and a Zenodo archive. The A5000 plus 125 GiB host RAM is exactly the target regime |
| `mlsys26-flexicache-leveraging-temporal-stability` | FlexiCache: Leveraging Temporal Stability of Attention Heads for Efficient KV Cache Management | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/94bcb01789fccf15afe2764d8fe0f40e-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/94bcb01789fccf15afe2764d8fe0f40e-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/NazmulTakbir/FlexiCache) | Hierarchical KV-cache manager on top of vLLM that classifies KV heads as stable or unstable and offloads cold pages to host memory; explicitly aimed at cutting GPU memory. Single GPU plus large host RAM is the ideal setup, and there is a Zenodo-archived artifact |

Concern - paper evaluates on heterogeneous multi-GPU setups (A100/A10G) and several models; scale-down to one 24 GB A5000 with a 7B model is straightforward but changes the SLO-attainment numbers. Python repo, actively pushed through 2025. |
| `socc25-adaspec-adaptive-speculative-decoding-fo` | AdaSpec: Adaptive Speculative Decoding for Fast, SLO-Aware Large Language Model Serving | SOCC 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3772052.3772239) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.05096) | [repo](https://github.com/cerebellumking/AdaSpec) | Adaptive draft length, confidence-prior verification and an SLO-aware efficiency estimator - all scheduling logic inside a single-GPU serving loop. Draft plus target model pair fits 24 GB. Ampere supports FlashAttention-2 so no FP8 dependence. Concern - trace replay assumes a certain request rate, may need rescaling. |
| `socc25-oneiros-kv-cache-optimization-through-pa` | Oneiros: KV Cache Optimization through Parameter Remapping for Multi-tenant LLM Serving | SOCC 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3772052.3772215) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.11507) | [repo](https://github.com/UT-SysML/Oneiros) | Remaps memory holding model parameters into KV cache instead of swapping; pure user-space change on top of vLLM. A 7-8B FP16 model plus KV cache fits the 24 GB A5000. Concern - the multi-tenant results assume several resident models, so the study must be scaled down to small models or quantized ones. Also check that the CUDA 12.x wheels it needs run on driver 535. |
| `sosp23-efficient-memory-management-for-large-la` | Efficient Memory Management for Large Language Model Serving with PagedAttention (vLLM) | SOSP 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3600006.3613165) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.06180) | [repo](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm) | KV-cache paging plus continuous batching; the canonical single-GPU LLM serving substrate. Runs entirely in user space on one CUDA GPU. Fits the A5000 with 7B FP16 models. Concerns - paper evaluates OPT-13B/66B/175B on multi-GPU A100 nodes so absolute numbers need scale-down to 7B-class; modern vLLM main branch has drifted far from the SOSP artifact (AE snapshot is https://github.com/sosp-ae-39/sosp-ae-astra) so pinning a version matters; recent wheels may want CUDA >12.2 which is a driver-535 risk |
| `sosp24-powerinfer-fast-large-language-model-ser` | PowerInfer: Fast Large Language Model Serving with a Consumer-grade GPU | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695964) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.12456) | [repo](https://github.com/SJTU-IPADS/PowerInfer) | Designed for exactly this machine class: one consumer/workstation GPU plus CPU, hot/cold neuron-aware GPU-CPU hybrid inference with offloading. llama.cpp-based, user-space build, CUDA 11.8/12.x friendly, no root. 7B-13B models in the sparsity-aware formats fit 24 GB or spill to the 125 GiB host RAM by design. Strongest single candidate. |
| `sosp25-aegaeon-effective-gpu-pooling-for-concur` | Aegaeon: Effective GPU Pooling for Concurrent LLM Serving on the Market | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764815) | [pdf](https://ennanzhai.github.io/pub/sosp25-aegaeon.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Zyyeric/Aegaeon) | The core mechanism - token-granularity auto-scaling that multiplexes many models onto *one* GPU with fast weight swapping - is inherently a single-GPU idea and the paper itself includes a single-A10 experiment. Official artifact (first author Y. Xiang; also Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.16673199). Concerns - the AE README asks for 2x8 H100 nodes, an A100-80GB node, ~512 GB CPU RAM and ~256 GB of model weights; a serious scale-down to a pool of small models on 24 GB is mandatory and the multi-node baselines cannot be reproduced. |
| `sosp25-diffkv-differentiated-memory-management` | DiffKV: Differentiated Memory Management for Large Language Models with Parallel KV Compaction | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764810) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.03131) | [repo](https://github.com/zyqCSL/DiffKV) | KV-cache compression plus an on-GPU allocator that compacts fragmented free lists in parallel; a vLLM-style single-GPU serving artifact and a very clean target for a "better allocator / better compression policy" hypothesis. Concerns - custom CUDA kernels may need a toolkit newer than the system nvcc 11.8 (installable per-user via conda, but driver 535 caps usable CUDA at 12.2). |
| `sosp25-hedrarag-co-optimizing-generation-and-re` | HedraRAG: Co-Optimizing Generation and Retrieval for Heterogeneous RAG Workflows | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764806) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3731569.3764806) | [repo](https://github.com/Leo9660/HedraRAG_AE) | Graph-based scheduling of RAG stages onto a single CPU-GPU pipeline - exactly the single-node, single-GPU setting of this machine. Concerns - retrieval index and corpus size must fit in 257 GB of free disk; generator model must be scaled to 7-8B FP16 to fit 24 GB. |
| `sosp25-jenga-effective-memory-management-for-se` | Jenga: Effective Memory Management for Serving LLM with Heterogeneity | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764823) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.18292) | [repo](https://github.com/heheda12345/Jenga-SOSP25-AE) | An attention-property-aware KV allocator implemented inside vLLM; single-GPU memory-utilization and throughput claims are directly measurable here, and the allocation policy is easy to perturb for an improvement hypothesis. Concerns - several evaluated models (large MoE and multimodal) exceed 24 GB, so the model set must be scaled down; upstream vLLM has moved on, so the AE snapshot may need pinning. |
| `sosp25-ktransformers-unleashing-the-full-potent` | KTransformers: Unleashing the Full Potential of CPU/GPU Hybrid Inference for MoE Models | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764843) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3731569.3764843) | [repo](https://github.com/kvcache-ai/ktransformers) | A single-GPU-plus-large-DRAM system by construction - the exact shape of this machine (1x24 GB GPU, 125 GB RAM). The Expert Deferral mechanism and the async CPU-GPU scheduler are hardware-agnostic and directly improvable. Very actively maintained (19.5k stars). Concerns - the headline CPU kernels are Intel AMX-specific and this is an AMD Zen3 Threadripper with no AMX and no AVX-512, so the CPU-side speedups will not reproduce as published; and 671B DeepSeek-V3/R1 needs far more than 125 GB, so a smaller MoE (e.g. Mixtral-class or Qwen3-MoE) scale-down is required. |
| `sosp25-pie-a-programmable-serving-system-for-em` | Pie: A Programmable Serving System for Emerging LLM Applications | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764814) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.24051) | [repo](https://github.com/pie-project/pie) | Decomposes the generation loop into handlers driven by user WebAssembly "inferlets"; all sandboxing is user space and the evaluation model sizes fit one 24 GB GPU. Strong candidate for an improvement project (new KV-cache strategies are the paper's own stated extension point). Concerns - none major; needs a Rust toolchain installed in user space. |
| `sosp25-prefillonly-an-inference-engine-for-pref` | PrefillOnly: An Inference Engine for Prefill-only Workloads in Large Language Model Applications | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764834) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.07203) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.16972909) | Storing only the last layer's KV cache drastically cuts GPU memory, which is precisely what makes long-input LLM work feasible on a 24 GB card; JCT-aware scheduling (shortest-prefill-first) is an obvious axis to improve. Concerns - the official artifact is a Zenodo zip of a vLLM fork (vllm_prefill-bowen-artifact-eval.zip) rather than a maintained Git repository, so it will not track upstream vLLM. |

Offline batching is a natural single-GPU workload and scheduling ideas port down easily. Concern: it is a production study at Alibaba so the traces and the 7B-plus model fleet may not be released - the optimization part may still be reproducible on one GPU. |
| `sosp26-beyond-utilization-energy-conscious-gpu` | Beyond Utilization: Energy-Conscious GPU Sharing for Inference Serving | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | Energy and carbon-conscious spatial GPU sharing for inference serving. Single-GPU colocation is the exact setting. NVML power readings and MPS both work without root on this machine so the measurement methodology is reproducible. Concern: no preprint or artifact link found yet. |
| `sosp26-diflow-a-system-for-micro-serving-text-t` | DiFlow: A System for Micro-Serving Text-to-Image Diffusion Workflows | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08123) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.08123) | — | LegoDiffusion decomposes text-to-image diffusion workflows into independently scheduled model nodes. SD or FLUX class pipelines fit in 24 GB and the per-model sharing idea is testable on one GPU. Concerns: per-model scaling and adaptive model parallelism are cluster-scale claims that must be scaled down or simulated. No artifact link found. |
| `sosp26-janus-multi-llm-serving-at-production-sc` | Janus: Multi-LLM Serving at Production Scale | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://yangtonghome.github.io/uploads/Janus_SOSP2026.pdf) | [pdf](https://yangtonghome.github.io/uploads/Janus_SOSP2026.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Janus2026/Janus) | Multi-LLM serving with dual-timescale scheduling, HBM virtualization (xTensor) and device-to-device model fork. Repo exists. Big concern: deployed on 768 devices in production, so the elastic-scaling and bin-packing results have no direct single-GPU analogue - the request scheduler and xTensor memory virtualization do. |
| `sosp26-llm-42-enabling-determinism-in-llm-infer` | LLM-42: Enabling Determinism in LLM Inference with Verified Speculation | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17768) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.17768) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/llm-42) | Scheduling-based determinism for LLM inference using verified speculation instead of batch-invariant kernels. Runs on one GPU with a 7-8B model. Official Microsoft repo referenced from the preprint. One of the strongest candidates on this list for a 10-week improvement project. |
| `sosp26-lmtracer-fine-grained-and-real-time-perf` | LMTracer: Fine-Grained and Real-Time Performance Profiling for Production LLM Systems | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | Fine-grained real-time profiler for LLM serving systems. Profiling and tracing work is user space. Serious concern: GPU-side fine-grained profiling usually needs CUPTI, and on driver 535 CUPTI profiling counters are admin-restricted by default - plus perf_event_paranoid=3 blocks CPU counters here. No preprint or artifact found. |

Memory-efficient multi-model serving on one 24 GB GPU is precisely this machine. Concern: no preprint or artifact found yet. |
| `sosp26-scalable-context-orchestration-for-servi` | Scalable Context Orchestration for Serving LLMs Over Voice | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04288) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04288) | — | Context-management middleware for voice LLM serving - explicitly models speaking rate, noise and packet loss in the context it builds. ASR plus 7-8B LLM plus TTS fits on one A5000 and the middleware itself is CPU-side. Concern: no artifact URL in the preprint. |

| `atc24-tileclipper-lightweight-selection-of-reg` | TileClipper: Lightweight Selection of Regions of Interest from Videos for Traffic Surveillance | ATC 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc24/presentation/chaudhary) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc24-chaudhary.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/shubhamchdhary/TileClipper) | Video-analytics pipeline that selects tiles by bitrate before sending frames to a detector; artifact is user-space Python plus Kvazaar and YOLOv5 and runs on one GPU, with a clear bandwidth-versus-accuracy tradeoff to improve. Concern: the live-deployment numbers used Raspberry Pi and Jetson Nano edge devices that we do not have |
| `atc25-crosspipe-towards-optimal-pipeline-sched` | CrossPipe: Towards Optimal Pipeline Schedules for Cross-Datacenter Training | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/chen-tiancheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-chen-tiancheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/spcl/crosspipe) | Solver-based and greedy pipeline-schedule optimizer with an execution engine that separates scheduling from execution, so the schedule optimization can be studied in simulation. Concern: hardware evaluation used 4x GH200, so only the solver/simulation half is reproducible here |
| `atc25-identifying-and-analyzing-pitfalls-in-gn` | Identifying and Analyzing Pitfalls in GNN Systems | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/gong) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-gong.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/the-data-lab) | Reproduction-oriented analysis of accuracy and memory pitfalls in GNN systems, explicitly framed around single-GPU systems and run on one A100 40 GB. Concern: the paper only points at the lab GitHub org rather than a named GraphPy repo, so the exact artifact needs identification |
| `atc25-jenga-enhancing-llm-long-context-fine-tu` | JENGA: Enhancing LLM Long-Context Fine-tuning with Contextual Token Sparsity | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/wang-tuowei) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-wang-tuowei.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Pairshoe/Jenga-AE) | LLM fine-tuning system exploiting contextual token sparsity to cut activation memory; artifact minimum is a single A800 or A40 GPU so smaller OPT/Llama models fit the 24 GB A5000. Concern: scalability experiments used 4x 4090 and long-context runs may need memory scale-down |
| `atc25-save-software-implemented-fault-toleranc` | SAVE: Software-Implemented Fault Tolerance for Model Inference against GPU Memory Bit Flips | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/zheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-zheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/peterzheng98/gpu-memory) | Software fault tolerance that splits model inference state across reliable and unreliable GPU memory; single-GPU, user-space bit-flip injection, and it was already evaluated on consumer-class GPUs including a 4090. Concern: the reliable-memory abstraction assumes accelerator features that may need emulation on the A5000 |
| `atc25-spaceexit-enabling-efficient-adaptive-co` | SpaceExit: Enabling Efficient Adaptive Computing in Space with Early Exits | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/liu-jiacheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-liu-jiacheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/zhuxz0299/ATC-artifact-submission) | Early-exit adaptive inference plus resource scheduling for satellite imagery; the artifact lists an ordinary CUDA GPU (tested on RTX 4090) and a multi-core CPU. Concern: the paper also uses a Jetson Orin Nano testbed emulating CubeSat hardware that cannot be reproduced |
| `atc25-voltrix-sparse-matrix-matrix-multiplicat` | Voltrix: Sparse Matrix-Matrix Multiplication on Tensor Cores with Asynchronous and Balanced Kernel Optimization | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/xia) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-xia.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/YaqiXia/Voltrix-SpMM) | Tensor-core SpMM kernel with asynchronous pipelining and load balancing; single-GPU CUDA kernel work, Ampere supports cp.async and FA2-style pipelining. Concern: evaluated on an 80 GB A100, so the larger graph datasets must be trimmed to 24 GB |
| `eurosys23-mariusgnn-resource-efficient-out-of-core` | MariusGNN: Resource-Efficient Out-of-Core Training of Graph Neural Networks | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567501) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567501) | [repo](https://github.com/marius-team/marius) | Explicitly a single-machine single-GPU out-of-core GNN training system whose whole thesis is that one GPU plus SSD beats multi-GPU baselines. Mature open-source repo with the EuroSys'23 artifact tag. Concern - the largest evaluated graphs need hundreds of GB of disk; 257 GB free means smaller graphs only. |
| `eurosys23-refl-resource-efficient-federated-learni` | REFL: Resource-Efficient Federated Learning | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567485) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567485) | [repo](https://github.com/ahmedcs/REFL) | Participant-selection and staleness-aware aggregation policies evaluated in a single-node FL simulator (FedScale lineage) - a pure user-space Python/PyTorch artifact. Concern - the paper's simulations use many worker processes and small vision/NLP models; client counts and model sizes will need scaling down to one A5000. |
| `eurosys24-accelerating-privacy-preserving-machine` | Accelerating Privacy-Preserving Machine Learning With GeniBatch | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629563) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3629563) | [repo](https://github.com/Huangxy-Minel/GeniBatch) | A user-space compiler that rewrites PHE-based ML programs into batched (SIMD-packed) ciphertext programs; built on FATE, all CPU. Concern - cross-silo setting means two or more parties, but they can be run as separate processes on one host; the speedup claims are compute-bound, the traffic claims are not reproducible without a real link |
| `eurosys24-gmorph-accelerating-multi-dnn-inference` | GMorph: Accelerating Multi-DNN Inference via Model Fusion | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650074) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650074) | [repo](https://github.com/qizhengyang98/GMorph) | Graph-mutation search that fuses several pre-trained task-specific DNNs into one multi-task model for cheaper inference. Models are CNN/small-transformer scale and inference is single-GPU. Concern - the search phase is expensive (repeated fine-tuning), so the search budget may have to be cut down |
| `eurosys24-minuet-accelerating-3d-sparse-convolutio` | Minuet: Accelerating 3D Sparse Convolutions on GPUs | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629560) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.06145) | [repo](https://github.com/UofT-EcoSystem/Minuet) | Single-GPU sparse-convolution engine (segmented sort + binary search instead of hash tables, tile autotuning). Runs on one commodity GPU; point-cloud datasets are small. Concern - autotuned tile sizes were tuned for A100/V100 class GPUs so numbers will shift on an RTX A5000 with CUDA 11.8; that shift is itself a plausible improvement angle |
| `eurosys24-model-selection-for-latency-critical-inf` | Model Selection for Latency-Critical Inference Serving | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629565) | [pdf](https://cs.stanford.edu/~trippel/pubs/mendoza_EuroSys24.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/dmmendo/RAMSIS) | RAMSIS generates model-selection/scheduling policies from a statistical model of query arrivals; policy generation is pure CPU work and serving uses a small ImageNet model zoo that fits 24 GB. Concern - the paper's worker pool is emulated across several GPUs; a single-GPU or simulated-worker scale-down is needed |
| `eurosys24-wisegraph-optimizing-gnn-with-joint-work` | WiseGraph: Optimizing GNN with Joint Workload Partition of Graph and Operations | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650063) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650063) | [repo](https://github.com/xxcclong/CxGNN-Compute) | GNN training framework with a joint graph/operator partitioning abstraction (gTask). The paper reports separate single-GPU numbers (2.04x) alongside multi-GPU, so the mechanism has a genuine single-GPU meaning. Concern - the largest graphs in the paper may not fit 24 GB, so a smaller dataset subset is needed |
| `eurosys24-zkml-an-optimizing-system-for-ml-inferen` | ZKML: An Optimizing System for ML Inference in Zero-Knowledge Proofs | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650088) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650088) | [repo](https://github.com/ddkang/zkml) | An optimizing compiler from TensorFlow graphs to halo2 ZK-SNARK circuits - a user-space Rust/Python compiler with a clear performance objective (gadget layout choices swing performance 24x). | `eurosys25-groot-graph-centric-row-reordering-with` | Groot: Graph-Centric Row Reordering with Tree for Sparse Matrix Multiplications on Tensor Cores | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3717460) | — | [repo](https://github.com/yuang-chen/Groot-EuroSys25) | Row reordering as a preprocessing pass for SpMM and SDDMM on Tensor Cores; single GPU with standard sparse matrix and GNN datasets. | `eurosys25-hybridflow-a-flexible-and-efficient-rlhf` | HybridFlow: A Flexible and Efficient RLHF Framework | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3696075) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.19256) | [repo](https://github.com/volcengine/verl) | RLHF dataflow framework combining single-controller and multi-controller execution; verl runs end to end on one GPU with 0.5B to 1.5B models using FSDP plus vLLM, so the API and scheduling layer can be studied at reduced scale. | `eurosys26-automated-end-to-end-model-serving-with` | Automated End-to-End Model Serving with Cooperative Compilation and Scheduling | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3769392) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3769392) | — | Cooperative compilation and scheduling of multiple kernel variants for model serving - single-GPU compiler plus runtime contribution. Concern: multiple participants would have to be simulated sequentially on the one A5000 and no code release was found |

Concerns: million-scale corpora may strain the 257 GB of free disk, and no code was found |

Strong fit |

Concern: paper frames it as on-device FL |

No code found yet |
| `fast23-shade-enable-fundamental-cacheability-fo` | SHADE: Enable Fundamental Cacheability for Distributed Deep Learning Training | FAST 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast23/presentation/khan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast23-khan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/rkhan055/SHADE) | Importance-sampling-aware caching layer for the deep-learning training data pipeline, implemented in user space on top of PyTorch's DataLoader plus a key-value cache. Concern: the paper evaluates distributed DLT across multiple nodes and GPUs; here it would have to be scaled down to one node with one RTX A5000, which is a meaningful scale-down but changes the I/O-vs-compute balance the paper leans on. Also assumes a remote/slow backing store - that would have to be emulated locally (e.g. FUSE or cgroup I/O limits), which is doable without root. |
| `fast26-gpu-checkpoint-restore-made-fast-and-lig` | GPU Checkpoint/Restore Made Fast and Lightweight | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/zeng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-zeng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/thustorage/GCR) | Distinguished Artifact Award; single-GPU system-level checkpoint/restore via CUDA API interception and CPU shadow execution - the mechanism is single-GPU by construction. Concerns worth auditing: the cuda-checkpoint baseline needs a newer NVIDIA driver than the installed 535.216.01, and CRIU-style process checkpointing often needs elevated privileges, which are unavailable here. |
| `fast26-preparation-meets-opportunity-enhancing` | Preparation Meets Opportunity: Enhancing Data Preprocessing for ML Training With Seneca | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/desai) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-desai.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/swiftomkar/seneca-fast26-pytorch) | Cache partitioning + opportunistic sampling in the PyTorch data loader; user-space, storage-bound, one node. Concerns: datasets in the paper range 142 GB-1.4 TB vs ~257 GB free, so a smaller dataset is needed; the paper's multi-node / NVLink configurations are out of reach, and a single A5000 limits how many concurrent training jobs can be co-run. |
| `middleware23-characterizing-distributed-machine-learn` | Characterizing Distributed Machine Learning Workloads on Apache Spark (Experimentation and Deployment Paper) | MIDDLEWARE 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3590140.3629112) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3590140.3629112) | [repo](https://github.com/DMLCharacterization/DMLCharacterization) | Studies the interplay of Spark platform parameters vs. ML hyper-parameters over 13 learning methods and 6 datasets; the headline claim (platform parameters matter more than hyper-parameters for model quality) is a clean testable hypothesis and Spark MLlib is pure user-space JVM code that runs in local/standalone mode on 16 cores and 125 GiB RAM. Concerns - the paper is a characterization study run on two multi-node clusters so absolute numbers will not reproduce on one node; the released artifact is mostly workload scripts plus traces rather than a system to improve; an improvement angle would have to be an auto-tuner built on their traces. Traces are also archived separately (Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.14151075) |
| `middleware24-amalgam-a-framework-for-obfuscated-neura` | Amalgam: A Framework for Obfuscated Neural Network Training on the Cloud | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700762) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.03405) | [repo](https://github.com/SifatTaj/amalgam) | Model/dataset augmentation for obfuscated training; repo URL is printed in the abstract. Vision and NLP models used are modest and fit a single 24 GB A5000; the "cloud" framing is only a deployment story and the measured quantity is per-GPU training overhead, which reproduces locally. |
| `middleware24-deep-optimizer-states-towards-scalable-t` | Deep Optimizer States: Towards Scalable Training of Transformer Models Using Interleaved Offloading | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700781) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.21316) | [repo](https://github.com/DataStates/artifacts) | Mechanism is CPU-GPU interleaved offloading of optimizer state driven by intra-iteration GPU memory fluctuation, which is meaningful with one GPU plus large host RAM - exactly this machine's shape (24 GB GPU, 125 GiB RAM). Artifact URL is printed in the paper and the directory contains run scripts and sample logs. Concern: the paper evaluates 7B-70B models across many GPUs, so a scale-down to a ~1B-class model is required; the DataStates DeepSpeed/Megatron-DeepSpeed forks may want a CUDA newer than the system 11.8, which conda can supply within driver 535's compatibility window. Merge note: scout linked the subdirectory https://github.com/DataStates/artifacts/tree/main/deep-optimizer-states . |
| `middleware24-quickdrop-efficient-federated-unlearning` | QuickDrop: Efficient Federated Unlearning via Synthetic Data Generation | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700764) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.15603) | [repo](https://github.com/sacs-epfl/quickdrop) | Headline metric is unlearning wall-clock time (463x vs retraining), i.e. a systems-efficiency claim, and FL clients are simulated as processes on one machine over CIFAR-scale datasets. Repo description names this Middleware'24 paper. Concern: the arXiv preprint appears under the earlier title "Efficient Federated Unlearning by Integrated Dataset Distillation". |
| `middleware24-roleml-a-role-oriented-programming-model` | RoleML: a Role-Oriented Programming Model for Customizable Distributed Machine Learning on Edges | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700765) | — | [repo](https://github.com/mrtan-ys/RoleML) | Python programming model plus runtime; roles are ordinary processes and can be co-located on a single host, and the paper's headline claim is low runtime overhead of the abstraction. Repo is by the first author. Concern: the automatic role-offloading mechanism is built on containerization and Docker/Podman are not installed, so that sub-feature would need porting to plain processes or bwrap. |
| `middleware25-freeride-harvesting-bubbles-in-pipeline` | FreeRide: Harvesting Bubbles in Pipeline Parallelism | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3730950) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.06941) | [repo](https://github.com/jiashu-z/freeride) | Middleware that detects idle pipeline-parallel bubbles and schedules generic GPU side tasks into them with MPS-based resource capping. Concern: evaluated on 4x RTX 6000 Ada (48 GB) plus a second server; pipeline parallelism needs >=2 stages, so a single-GPU scale-down means emulating 2 stages as separate processes on the one A5000 with a small model (nanoGPT-class). Concern: the side-task isolation path uses Docker, which is not installed and would need replacing by cgroup v2 + MPS (both usable unprivileged here). Core mechanism itself is per-GPU and user space. |
| `middleware25-xmem-a-cpu-based-approach-for-accurate-e` | xMem: A CPU-Based Approach for Accurate Estimation of GPU Memory in Deep Learning Training Workloads | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770773) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.21048) | [repo](https://github.com/Stone-ResearchLife/xMem) | The estimator itself runs CPU-only (dynamic analysis of allocator behaviour), so the expensive half of the pipeline needs no GPU at all; the single A5000 is only needed to collect ground-truth peak memory. Their 25-model suite is CNN/Transformer training, most of which fits in 24 GB at reduced batch size. Concern: a few of the 25 models may not fit in 24 GB, so the validation set will have to be trimmed; 5209 runs is a lot of wall-clock on one GPU and would need subsampling. |
| `mlsys23-alcop-automatic-load-compute-pipelining` | ALCOP: Automatic Load-Compute Pipelining in Deep Learning Compiler for AI-GPUs | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/d6cde2c1b161daa31be560d062cf2251-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/d6cde2c1b161daa31be560d062cf2251-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/hgyhungry/alcop-artifact) | Compiler-native multi-stage multi-level load-compute pipelining in TVM plus an analytic performance model for schedule search - entirely single-GPU kernel generation - concern - pinned to an older TVM and CUDA and the pipelining depth assumptions target A100 so Ampere consumer retuning is part of the work |
| `mlsys23-approxcaliper-a-programmable-framework-f` | ApproxCaliper: A Programmable Framework for Application-aware Neural Network Optimization | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/89efa87dc8f0a5d18e4ae0a479658f60-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/89efa87dc8f0a5d18e4ae0a479658f60-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/uiuc-arc/approxcaliper) | Framework that prunes and quantizes networks against application-level goals rather than accuracy alone - the optimization pipeline runs on one GPU - concern - two of the case studies are a commercial agriculture robot and an electric-cart simulation so the application-level feedback loop may not be fully reproducible |
| `mlsys23-cuttlefish-low-rank-model-training-witho` | Cuttlefish: Low-Rank Model Training without All the Tuning | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/c2db3ef0b1ad4c5ec7c3a0a0c6f6c832-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/c2db3ef0b1ad4c5ec7c3a0a0c6f6c832-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/hwang595/Cuttlefish) | Automated low-rank training that picks per-layer ranks from observed stable rank - end-to-end training runs on one GPU on CIFAR or ImageNet-scale vision and small transformer models - concern - the contribution is closer to a training method than a systems artifact and full ImageNet training may exceed the time budget |
| `mlsys23-efficient-gpu-kernels-for-n-m-sparse-wei` | Efficient GPU Kernels for N:M-Sparse Weights in Deep Learning | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/a10deb4d5227a8ea307ea8ff3cb712f4-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/a10deb4d5227a8ea307ea8ff3cb712f4-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/SparTA) | GPU SpMV and SpMM kernels for general N to M sparse weights - pure single-GPU kernel work in the nmsparse_artifact branch of SparTA - concern - evaluated on A100 and the artifact uses Docker so it needs a user-space rebuild and retuning for Ampere consumer cards. Merge note: scout linked the branch https://github.com/microsoft/SparTA/tree/nmsparse_artifact . |
| `mlsys23-exploiting-hardware-utilization-and-adap` | Exploiting Hardware Utilization and Adaptive Dataflow for Efficient Sparse Convolution in 3D Point Clouds | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/8e3e27d07b3f663b7f85c819b9692163-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/8e3e27d07b3f663b7f85c819b9692163-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/hkeee21/PCEngine) | Sparse-convolution engine for 3D point clouds with a coded-CSR format and adaptive dataflow - single-GPU kernel work with released code - concern - point-cloud datasets such as SemanticKITTI are large and the CUDA code targets older toolkits |
| `mlsys23-fedtree-a-federated-learning-system-for` | FedTree: A Federated Learning System For Trees | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/3430e7055936cb8e26451ed49fce84a6-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/3430e7055936cb8e26451ed49fce84a6-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Xtra-Computing/FedTree) | Tree-based federated GBDT training system in C++ or CUDA - parties are simulated as processes on one host and the datasets are small - concern - homomorphic-encryption modes are slow and the secure-aggregation parts add build dependencies |
| `mlsys23-gluefl-reconciling-client-sampling-and-m` | GlueFL: Reconciling Client Sampling and Model Masking for Bandwidth Efficient Federated Learning | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/da5efc7c4014940f21bcb7fdfd4efab1-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/da5efc7c4014940f21bcb7fdfd4efab1-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/TCtower/GlueFL) | Federated training framework built on FedScale that combines sticky client sampling with bounded-change compression masks - the evaluation is trace-driven FedScale simulation which fits one GPU - concern - the contribution is mostly algorithmic and the reported runs used a multi-GPU simulation cluster so rounds must be scaled down |
| `mlsys23-hypergef-a-framework-enabling-efficient` | HyperGef: A Framework Enabling Efficient Fusion for Hypergraph Neural Network on GPUs | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/895783891e093ded4f0c48c250fde92a-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/895783891e093ded4f0c48c250fde92a-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/fishmingyu/HyperGef) | Fused GPU kernels and a workload-aware sparse format for hypergraph neural networks - single-GPU kernel work compared against cuSPARSE - concern - graph datasets must be sized to 24 GB and the kernels may need retuning for Ampere consumer-class shared memory |
| `mlsys23-megablocks-efficient-sparse-training-wit` | MegaBlocks: Efficient Sparse Training with Mixture-of-Experts | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/5a54f79333768effe7e8927bcccffe40-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/5a54f79333768effe7e8927bcccffe40-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/stanford-futuredata/megablocks) | Block-sparse GPU kernels for MoE layers - the kernels and the no-token-dropping formulation are single-GPU contributions and the artifact claims a 30 second microbenchmark - concern - paper uses A100 and Docker so it needs a user-space rebuild and the Ampere A5000 has less bandwidth and no A100-class shared memory |
| `mlsys23-renee-end-to-end-training-of-extreme-cla` | Renee: END-TO-END TRAINING OF EXTREME CLASSIFICATION MODELS | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/c62748872b8c4c49799fc0fa0878515f-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/c62748872b8c4c49799fc0fa0878515f-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/renee) | End-to-end training system for extreme classification with GPU memory and throughput optimizations - the paper runs on one to four A100s and smaller XC benchmarks fit a single 24 GB GPU - concern - the largest label sets in the paper will not fit so scale-down to medium benchmarks is required |
| `mlsys23-sirius-harvesting-whole-program-optimiza` | SIRIUS: Harvesting Whole-Program Optimization Opportunities for DNNs | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/3e4e24f7e055320fa54c03f6e816775f-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/3e4e24f7e055320fa54c03f6e816775f-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://zenodo.org/record/7885573) | Whole-program polyhedral compiler fusing host and kernel code for DNN inference on one NVIDIA GPU - single-GPU evaluation against TensorRT and TVM - concern - the artifact appendix ships the source inside a Docker image mirrored on Zenodo and on Docker Hub as sunqianqi/sirius - Docker is not installed here so it must be rebuilt from source with LLVM 16 and PipLib in user space - the Zenodo record did not respond when checked on 2026-09-13 |
| `mlsys23-unified-convolution-framework-a-compiler` | Unified Convolution Framework: A compiler-based approach to support sparse convolutions | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/ccf7262fb986e4367ccd3903960c57a0-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/ccf7262fb986e4367ccd3903960c57a0-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/nullplay/Unified-Convolution-Framework) | TACO-based compiler that expresses many sparse convolution variants through affine index expressions - CPU and single-GPU code generation with a Zenodo and GitHub artifact - concern - builds a TACO fork with cmake and LLVM which is a user-space build but not a trivial one |
| `mlsys24-acrobat-optimizing-auto-batching-of-dyna` | ACROBAT: Optimizing Auto-batching of Dynamic Deep Learning at Compile Time | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/096b1019463f34eb241e87cfce8dfe16-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/096b1019463f34eb241e87cfce8dfe16-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/pratikfegade/acrobat_code) | Compiler + runtime for auto-batching dynamic DNNs; paper's own evaluation is a single RTX 3070, so the machine is a superset. Concern - no code URL in the paper; the repo is the first author's undocumented TVM fork (last push 2022), so a from-source TVM/CUDA build is a real risk, and baselines DyNet/Cavs must also be built. |
| `mlsys24-proteus-preserving-model-confidentiality` | Proteus: Preserving Model Confidentiality during Graph Optimizations | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/c66a9db149261435664284a20b6f1d42-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/c66a9db149261435664284a20b6f1d42-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/proteus-mlsys24/mlsys24-artifact) | Partitions a DNN computation graph and hides subgraphs among generated decoys so a third party can still run compiler/graph optimizations - a user-space compiler artifact (artifact repo published, with a Dockerfile that can be read and reproduced via conda). Concern - the headline metric is confidentiality, not speed, so a performance hypothesis has to target obfuscation cost or preserved optimization quality; measuring the latter means running autotuning, which is GPU-hour heavy. |
| `mlsys24-vqpy-an-object-oriented-approach-to-mode` | VQPy: An Object-Oriented Approach to Modern Video Analytics | MLSYS 2024 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/87eaaa8605a1a472d9a9756e7500517b-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2024/file/87eaaa8605a1a472d9a9756e7500517b-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/vqpy/vqpy) | Python front-end plus an extensible backend that builds and optimizes video-analytics pipelines (detector/tracker cascades, frame filtering) - entirely user space, one GPU. Concern - reproducing the query set needs public video datasets and YOLO-class weights; gains are pipeline-optimization gains, so a hypothesis must be framed around the backend optimizer. |
| `mlsys25-apollo-sgd-like-memory-adamw-level-perfo` | APOLLO: SGD-like Memory, AdamW-level Performance | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/437bc4ccafd3fc6d4289bd10940be42b-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/437bc4ccafd3fc6d4289bd10940be42b-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/zhuhanqing/APOLLO) | Memory-efficient optimizer - drop-in AdamW replacement, 60M-1B LLaMA pre-training runs fit one 24 GB GPU and memory savings are directly measurable - concern: headline 7B numbers need multi-GPU |
| `mlsys25-adaparse-an-adaptive-parallel-pdf-parsin` | AdaParse: An Adaptive Parallel PDF Parsing and Resource Scaling Engine | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/678773d96b5822e93348aeb5c80d4dc5-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/678773d96b5822e93348aeb5c80d4dc5-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/7shoe/AdaParse) | Adaptive PDF-parsing and resource-scaling engine for building training corpora - user-space Python parsers plus a learned parser selector - concern: paper scales on an HPC cluster, so single-node runs need a reduced document set |
| `mlsys25-fedprophet-memory-efficient-federated-ad` | FedProphet: Memory-Efficient Federated Adversarial Training via Robust and Consistent Cascade Learning | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/96f39c8de84678cb2a908cd52bfd7819-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/96f39c8de84678cb2a908cd52bfd7819-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Yoruko-Tang/FedProphet) | Memory-efficient cascade training for federated adversarial training - evaluation is a single-machine FL simulation with per-device memory budgets - concern: more an algorithm/system hybrid, and edge-device memory claims are emulated rather than measured on real devices |
| `mlsys25-flexattention-a-programming-model-for-ge` | FlexAttention: A Programming Model for Generating Fused Attention Variants. | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/61a9278dfef5f871b5e472389f8d6fa1-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/61a9278dfef5f871b5e472389f8d6fa1-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/meta-pytorch/attention-gym) | Compiler-driven programming model for fused attention variants - shipped in PyTorch core with attention-gym as the paper artifact, single GPU - concern: Ampere means no FP8 variants and torch.compile kernel quality differs from H100 numbers |
| `mlsys25-graph-learning-at-scale-characterizing-a` | Graph Learning at Scale: Characterizing and Optimizing Pre-Propagation GNNs | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/0badcb4e95306df76a719409155e46e8-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/0badcb4e95306df76a719409155e46e8-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/cornell-zhang/preprop-gnn) | GNN training-system characterization plus data-loading and training optimizations - artifact is user-space PyTorch/DGL and the PP-GNN design decouples propagation so a single A5000 is enough for medium graphs - concern: largest OGB graphs need tens of GB of disk and host RAM |
| `mlsys25-hyc-lora-memory-efficient-lora-fine-tuni` | HyC-LoRA: Memory Efficient LoRA Fine-tuning with Hybrid Activation Compression | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/5431dca75a8d2abc1fb51e89e8324f10-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/5431dca75a8d2abc1fb51e89e8324f10-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/thu-ee-acts-lab/HyC-LoRA-release) | Activation-compression LoRA fine-tuning - explicitly targets single memory-constrained devices, 7B LoRA with 2-bit buffered activations fits 24 GB - Triton kernels, user space |
| `mlsys25-sparsetransx-efficient-training-of-trans` | SparseTransX: Efficient Training of Translation-Based Knowledge Graph Embeddings Using Sparse Matrix Operations | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/36e2967f87c3362e37cf988781a887ad-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/36e2967f87c3362e37cf988781a887ad-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/HipGraph/SpTransX) | Replaces scatter/gather embedding kernels with SpMM in knowledge-graph embedding training - CPU and single-GPU results, low GPU memory footprint - good fit for one node |
| `mlsys26-approxmlir-accuracy-aware-compiler-for-c` | ApproxMLIR : Accuracy-Aware Compiler for Compound ML System | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/bbd3e0e9913824bbc46e7e87b11461ae-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/bbd3e0e9913824bbc46e7e87b11461ae-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/uiuc-arc/approxMLIR) | Accuracy-aware MLIR dialect and optimizer for compound AI systems; the whole toolchain is user-space and MLIR/LLVM can be built into HOME. | `mlsys26-dynaflow-transparent-and-flexible-intra` | DynaFlow: Transparent and Flexible Intra-Device Parallelism via Programmable Operator Scheduling | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/bbd7d8bd780fcf7143add2317ba04638-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/bbd7d8bd780fcf7143add2317ba04638-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/uw-syfi/DynaFlow) | Intra-device parallelism via programmable operator scheduling - by construction a single-GPU contribution, integrated into six ML systems with released code, and compatible with CUDA Graphs and TorchInductor. Concerns: the paper only states ETC was 'incorporated into a major open-source system' without naming a repository, so the code location is unknown |
| `mlsys26-executorch-a-unified-pytorch-solution-to` | ExecuTorch - A Unified PyTorch Solution to Run ML Models On-Device | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/236f915dd02af4f11927f67330b21d4b-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/236f915dd02af4f11927f67330b21d4b-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/pytorch/executorch) | PyTorch-native on-device runtime - memory planning, operator/backend registry, quantization workflows are all user-space and buildable from source. Concerns: most of the evaluation is on phones, NPUs and MCUs that are unavailable; only the portable/XNNPACK/CUDA backends can be exercised here |
| `mlsys26-flashinfer-bench-building-the-virtuous-c` | FlashInfer-Bench: Building the Virtuous Cycle for AI-driven LLM Systems | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/37e44c4b5321605735be9761f9b758fc-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/37e44c4b5321605735be9761f9b758fc-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer-bench) | Closed-loop harness for LLM-generated GPU kernels with correctness/performance checks and hot-swap into vLLM or SGLang; a single GPU is enough to run the loop. Concerns: several curated traces and reference kernels assume Hopper-class features that Ampere lacks, and agent runs need paid LLM API budget |
| `mlsys26-flashlight-pytorch-compiler-extensions-t` | Flashlight: PyTorch Compiler Extensions to Accelerate Attention Variants | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/bc52716d13d2d72ea0f335667d86c0f8-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/bc52716d13d2d72ea0f335667d86c0f8-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/bozhiyou/pytorch-flashlight) | PyTorch compiler extension that auto-generates fused FlashAttention-style kernels for arbitrary attention variants, compared against FlexAttention. Single GPU; FlashAttention-2 style kernels are supported on Ampere |
| `mlsys26-grinnder-breaking-the-memory-capacity-wa` | GriNNder: Breaking the Memory Capacity Wall in Full-Graph GNN Training with Storage Offloading | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/215597a92341c78cc3e7fe628be4a195-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/215597a92341c78cc3e7fe628be4a195-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/AIS-SNU/GriNNder) | Full-graph GNN training on a single server by offloading to NVMe SSDs - designed exactly for the limited-GPU-memory single-node setting. Concerns: NVMe is software RAID and shared and only ~257 GB is free so the largest graphs will not fit; uses kvikio/GPUDirect-style paths that may fall back to buffered I/O without privileges |
| `mlsys26-mlcommons-chakra-advancing-performance-b` | MLCommons Chakra: Advancing Performance Benchmarking and Co-design using Standardized Execution Traces | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/53fe824f289060ce705ed7c01dae59d2-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/53fe824f289060ce705ed7c01dae59d2-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mlcommons/chakra) | Open trace schema plus collection/generation/replay tooling with an mlsys26 branch and Zenodo archive; trace analysis and ASTRA-sim style simulation run on one machine. Concerns: the traces themselves come from large production clusters, so any new work here is simulation- or replay-based rather than measured |
| `mlsys26-massive-scale-out-of-core-umap-on-the-gp` | Massive-Scale Out-Of-Core UMAP on the GPU | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/0badcb4e95306df76a719409155e46e8-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/0badcb4e95306df76a719409155e46e8-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/rapidsai/cuml) | Out-of-core GPU UMAP with an explicit single-GPU result (22.7x); batching/spilling strategy is the contribution and is squarely user-space CUDA. Concerns: multi-GPU is optional but is where the headline 74x comes from and the largest datasets exceed the ~257 GB free on /home so scale-down is needed |
| `mlsys26-pylo-towards-accessible-learned-optimize` | Pylo: Towards Accessible Learned Optimizers in PyTorch | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/6b1d4c03391b0aa6ddde0b807a78c950-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/6b1d4c03391b0aa6ddde0b807a78c950-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Belilovsky-Lab/pylo) | CUDA-accelerated learned-optimizer kernels exposed through torch.optim; the reported throughput gains are single-GPU ViT-B/16 training numbers. Concern: the large-scale pre-training claims assume more compute than one A5000 |
| `mlsys26-spira-exploiting-voxel-data-structural-p` | Spira: Exploiting Voxel Data Structural Properties for Efficient Sparse Convolution in Point Cloud Networks | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/7972f3735e104a54715922aa416fde1b-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/7972f3735e104a54715922aa416fde1b-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SPIN-Research-Group/Spira) | Single-GPU sparse-convolution engine for point-cloud networks with released code and clear baselines (torchsparse, spconv). Kernel-map construction is a self-contained, improvable CUDA artifact |
| `mlsys26-streamdiffusionv2-a-streaming-system-for` | StreamDiffusionV2: A Streaming System for Dynamic and Interactive Video Generation | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/698cfaf72a208aef2e78bcac55b74328-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/698cfaf72a208aef2e78bcac55b74328-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/chenfengxu714/StreamDiffusionV2) | Training-free streaming video-diffusion serving with an SLO-aware batching scheduler, block scheduler and rolling KV cache; the scheduling core runs on one GPU. Concern: the near-linear FPS scaling claim depends on multi-GPU pipeline orchestration that cannot be reproduced here |

Ampere is a supported target |
| `mlsys26-wave-a-symbolic-python-dsl-and-compiler` | Wave: A Symbolic Python DSL And Compiler for High-Performance Machine Learning | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/48c34730ff9a8574481a00ce8cb5e2cb-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/48c34730ff9a8574481a00ce8cb5e2cb-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/iree-org/wave) | Python-embedded kernel DSL and compiler that automates matrix-core address computation; open source under iree-org and buildable in user space. Concerns: the primary backend is AMD CDNA, so NVIDIA Ampere support is the risk to check first, and the toolchain needs a user-space LLVM/MLIR build |
