| `nsdi23-boggart-towards-general-purpose-accelera` | Boggart: Towards General-Purpose Acceleration of Retrospective Video Analytics | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/agarwal-neil) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-agarwal-neil.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/neilsagarwal/boggart) | Retrospective video analytics accelerator combining classical CV indices with sparing CNN invocation - runs as user-space Python on one GPU - Concern: needs large video corpora to be downloaded into the 257 GB of free space and depends on older CUDA-era PyTorch and OpenCV builds |
| `nsdi23-gemel-model-merging-for-memory-efficient` | Gemel: Model Merging for Memory-Efficient, Real-Time Video Analytics at the Edge | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/padmanabhan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-padmanabhan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/artpad6/gemel_nsdi23) | Model merging to cut GPU memory for concurrently served edge vision models - a pure user-space memory-management question that fits one 24 GB GPU - Concern: the target is memory-starved edge boxes so the memory budget has to be deliberately constrained on an A5000 and the repo is a research prototype |
| `nsdi23-modelkeeper-accelerating-dnn-training-vi` | ModelKeeper: Accelerating DNN Training via Automated Training Warmup | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/lai-fan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-lai-fan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SymbioticLab/ModelKeeper) | Training warmup by transforming weights of previously trained models - the matching plus transformation logic is CPU work and warm-started training of small models fits one 24 GB GPU - Concern: the paper uses a large shared cluster and a big model zoo so the zoo and datasets must be scaled down |
| `nsdi23-zeus-understanding-and-optimizing-gpu-en` | Zeus: Understanding and Optimizing GPU Energy Consumption of DNN Training | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/you) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-you.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ml-energy/zeus) | Energy-vs-time tradeoff for recurring DNN training jobs measured with just-in-time profiling on a single GPU - Concern: setting GPU power limits through NVML normally requires root so only the batch-size and job-level knobs may be reachable without the Zeus daemon - repo moved from SymbioticLab/Zeus to ml-energy/zeus |
| `nsdi24-fast-vector-query-processing-for-large-d` | Fast Vector Query Processing for Large Datasets Beyond GPU Memory with Reordered Pipelining | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/zhang-zili-pipelining) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-zhang-zili-pipelining.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/pkusys/Rummy) | GPU-accelerated billion-scale ANN vector search; the paper itself uses a single A100 and streams vectors from host RAM, so the mechanism is single-GPU. User-space CUDA on a Faiss fork. Concern: paper used 40 GB GPU and 1152 GB host RAM for billion-scale sets - on 24 GB GPU plus 125 GiB RAM and 257 GB free disk the study must scale down to 100M-scale datasets. CUDA toolkit newer than 11.8 would need a conda install. |
| `nsdi24-madeye-boosting-live-video-analytics-acc` | MadEye: Boosting Live Video Analytics Accuracy with Adaptive Camera Configurations | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/wong) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-wong.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/michaeldwong/madeye) | Live video analytics that adapts PTZ camera orientation; the released repo is trace-driven simulation code plus collected per-orientation datasets, and server-side inference (YOLO-class models) fits the A5000. Concerns: original setup needs physical PTZ cameras and Mahimahi link emulation (Mahimahi needs setuid/root), so work must stay inside the released simulator; datasets are hosted on Dropbox which is an availability risk. |
| `nsdi25-accelerating-design-space-exploration-fo` | Accelerating Design Space Exploration for LLM Training Systems with Multi-experiment Parallel Simulation | NSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi25/presentation/gui) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi25-gui.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/NASP-THU/multiverse) | Multiverse is a GPU-accelerated LLM-training simulator; the artifact README explicitly retains CPU-only execution, and the whole workload is simulation, so no real GPU cluster is needed. One A5000 plus 125 GiB RAM is a plausible platform, and simulator throughput/fidelity is an obvious improvement target. Concern: repo is at "Multiverse 2.0" which may have diverged from the NSDI '25 artifact; input traces are Chakra files that must be sourced. |
| `nsdi25-bytecheckpoint-a-unified-checkpointing-s` | ByteCheckpoint: A Unified Checkpointing System for Large Foundation Model Development | NSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi25/presentation/wan-borui) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi25-wan-borui.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/ByteCheckpoint) | Checkpoint save/load/resharding is fundamentally an I/O and serialization problem; the repo is a pip-installable PyTorch library with unit tests and demos. Ranks can be emulated with several processes over gloo/NCCL on one GPU or on CPU, and ~257 GB of NVMe is enough for sub-1B-parameter checkpoints. Concerns: the paper's headline numbers come from thousands of GPUs and remote object storage; single-node scale-down measures the resharding/serialization path only, not cluster-wide save throughput. |
| `nsdi25-region-based-content-enhancement-for-eff` | Region-based Content Enhancement for Efficient Video Analytics at the Edge | NSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi25/presentation/wang-weijun) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi25-wang-weijun.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mi150/RegenHance) | RegenHance is a PyTorch + PyCUDA pipeline (importance prediction, region stitching, super-resolution, detection) that runs on a single GPU; an RTX A5000 is stronger than the edge devices used in the paper, so a scale-down is meaningful and accuracy/throughput are directly measurable. Concerns: the paper evaluates across five heterogeneous edge devices, which cannot be reproduced; the repo README admits it was not reorganized after submission, so build effort is uncertain. |
| `nsdi25-simai-unifying-architecture-design-and-p` | SimAI: Unifying Architecture Design and Performance Tuning for Large-Scale LLM Training | NSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi25/presentation/wang-xizheng-simai) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi25-wang-xizheng-simai.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/aliyun/SimAI) | Official Alibaba artifact (URL printed in the paper). The analytical and ns-3-backed simulation modes are CPU-only user-space builds, so "simulate a 1k-GPU cluster" costs no GPUs; accuracy-vs-speed of the simulator is a testable hypothesis. Concerns: the SimAI-Physical mode needs a real GPU/RDMA cluster and must be skipped; the build pulls a large ns-3/MPI toolchain that has to be installed under $HOME. |
| `nsdi26-ava-towards-agentic-video-analytics-with` | AVA: Towards Agentic Video Analytics with Vision Language Models | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/yan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-yan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/I-ESC/Project-Ava) | VLM-based agentic video analytics - event knowledge graph indexing plus agentic retrieval; code and the AVA-100 benchmark are both public and the pipeline is built on LMDeploy so a 7-8B VLM fits in 24 GB. Concern - long-video indexing over the full benchmark is compute-heavy and 24 GB limits VLM size. |

Concern - evaluated in a multi-tenant multi-GPU datacenter setting and uses NVIDIA Transformer Engine which may want a newer CUDA than driver 535 supports. |
| `nsdi26-phantora-maximizing-code-reuse-in-simula` | Phantora: Maximizing Code Reuse in Simulation-based Machine Learning System Performance Estimation | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/qin) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-qin.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/QDelta/Phantora) | Hybrid GPU-cluster simulator that runs unmodified ML frameworks and intercepts GPU/comm calls, explicitly designed so cluster performance can be estimated from a single GPU. Concern - it emulates each GPU server as a container and Docker/Podman are not installed, so the container layer must be ported to bwrap or plain processes. |
| `nsdi26-rollpacker-taming-long-tail-rollouts-for` | RollPacker: Taming Long-Tail Rollouts for RL Post-Training with Tail Batching | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/gao-wei) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-gao-wei.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Farrrrland/RollPacker) | Tail batching - a rollout scheduling policy for synchronous RL post-training; the policy itself is workload-level and can be studied with a 0.5-1.5B model on one GPU or replayed in simulation. Concern - the paper's speedups are measured on multi-GPU veRL and SGLang deployments of Qwen2.5 models. |
| `osdi23-cocktailer-analyzing-and-optimizing-dyna` | Cocktailer: Analyzing and Optimizing Dynamic Control Flow in Deep Learning | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/zhang-chen) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-zhang-chen.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/nnfusion) | DNN compiler that schedules control flow into accelerator kernels; evaluated on a single GPU. Zenodo mirror: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7856472 . Merge note: scout linked the artifact branch https://github.com/microsoft/nnfusion/tree/cocktailer_artifact . |
| `osdi23-einnet-optimizing-tensor-programs-with-d` | EINNET: Optimizing Tensor Programs with Derivation-Based Transformations | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/zheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-zheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/InfiniTensor/InfiniTensor) | Derivation-based tensor program optimizer; evaluated on single V100 and A100 GPUs, so an A5000 is a valid scaled-down target. Concern: reported speedups are hardware-specific, so a reproduction will produce different absolute numbers on Ampere consumer-class silicon. AE artifact: https://github.com/zhengly123/OSDI23-EinNet-AE . |

Merge note: scout linked the artifact branch https://github.com/microsoft/nnfusion/tree/osdi2023welder . |
| `osdi24-chameleonapi-automatic-and-efficient-cus` | ChameleonAPI: Automatic and Efficient Customization of Neural Networks for ML Applications | OSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/liu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-liu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/UChi-JCL/chameleonAPI) | Application-aware retraining/customization of neural networks behind ML APIs - single-GPU training and inference of modest models no root needed |

Merge note: scout linked the artifact branch https://github.com/microsoft/BitBLAS/tree/osdi24_ladder_artifact . |
| `osdi24-mononn-enabling-a-new-monolithic-optimiz` | MonoNN: Enabling a New Monolithic Optimization Space for Neural Network Inference Tasks on Modern GPU-Centric Architectures | OSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/zhuang) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-zhuang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/AlibabaResearch/mononn) | Monolithic whole-graph GPU kernel optimization for inference - single-GPU compiler work on NVIDIA hardware. Concern - the placement scheduler is evaluated at cluster scale and would need simulation and MIG partitioning is unavailable on an A5000 so spatial sharing must rely on MPS or CUDA streams |
| `osdi24-nnscaler-constraint-guided-parallelizati` | nnScaler: Constraint-Guided Parallelization Plan Generation for Deep Learning Training | OSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/lin-zhiqi) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-lin-zhiqi.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/nnscaler) | Search-space construction and plan generation for DNN training parallelization - the plan search compiler and cost model are user-space and can be studied on one GPU. Concern - end-to-end speedup claims need multiple GPUs so validation would rely on the cost model or single-GPU memory-saving plans |
| `osdi25-bayesian-code-diffusion-for-efficient-au` | Bayesian Code Diffusion for Efficient Automatic Deep Learning Program Optimization | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/jeong) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-jeong.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/eai-lab/BayesianCodeDiffusion) | Auto-tuning search strategy implemented inside TVM/Ansor; runs entirely in user space on one CPU + one GPU, which is the paper's own evaluation setting. Strong fit: hypothesis-friendly (search-efficiency vs. achieved latency). Concern: full auto-tuning sweeps are long-running, so the 10-week budget must fix a small model set. |
| `osdi25-kperfir-towards-a-open-and-compiler-cent` | KPerfIR: Towards a Open and Compiler-centric Ecosystem for GPU Kernel Performance Tooling on Modern AI Workloads | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/guan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-guan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ChandlerGuan/kperfir_artifact) | Profiling implemented as Triton compiler passes (upstreamed into triton-lang/triton third_party/proton/dialect); instrumentation is intra-kernel clock-based, so it does not need perf or root. Concern: the motivating use case is warp-specialized overlap on Hopper; on an Ampere A5000 some passes may be inapplicable, and Triton builds usually want CUDA 12.x installed via conda rather than the system 11.8. |
| `osdi25-mirage-a-multi-level-superoptimizer-for` | Mirage: A Multi-Level Superoptimizer for Tensor Programs | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/wu-mengdi) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-wu-mengdi.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mirage-project/mirage) | Single-GPU tensor-program superoptimizer, mature public repo, Available + Functional + Reproduced. Concern: the search plus Z3 probabilistic equivalence check is CPU/GPU heavy and some generated kernels target A100/H100 feature levels - expect to re-tune for sm_86 and 24 GB. |
| `osdi25-pipethreader-software-defined-pipelining` | PipeThreader: Software-Defined Pipelining for Efficient DNN Execution | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/cheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-cheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/tile-ai/tilelang) | DNN compiler with software-defined pipelining, open-sourced inside TileLang; single-GPU by construction (FlashAttention, Mamba2 kernels). Concerns: the headline motivation is the Hopper Tensor Memory Accelerator, which Ampere lacks - the A5000 can only exercise the TensorCore/async-copy subset; also note the repo is the whole TileLang project, so the PipeThreader-specific code must be located within it. |
| `osdi25-qimeng-xpiler-transcompiling-tensor-prog` | QiMeng-Xpiler: Transcompiling Tensor Programs for Deep Learning Systems with a Neural-Symbolic Approach | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/dong) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-dong.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/QiMeng-IPRC/QiMeng-Xpiler) | LLM + symbolic-synthesis transcompiler; the toolchain itself is user-space Python and the CUDA target is testable on the A5000. Concern: three of the four evaluated back-ends (Intel AVX512-VNNI, AMD MI HIP, Cambricon BANG) do not exist on this AMD Zen3 + NVIDIA machine, so only a subset of the translation matrix can be reproduced; also needs an LLM API budget. Available + Functional, not Reproduced. |
| `osdi25-training-with-confidence-catching-silent` | Training with Confidence: Catching Silent Errors in Deep Learning Training with Automated Proactive Checks | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/jiang) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-jiang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/OrderLab/TrainCheck) | TrainCheck is a Python instrumentation/invariant-inference layer over PyTorch; Available + Functional + Reproduced. Concern: a subset of the 20 reproduced silent-error cases involve DeepSpeed/multi-GPU configurations that cannot be recreated on one 24 GB GPU - the single-GPU subset is still substantial. |
| `osdi25-understanding-stragglers-in-large-model` | Understanding Stragglers in Large Model Training Using What-if Analysis | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/lin-jinkun) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-lin-jinkun.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/StragglerAnalysis) | The artifact is a trace-driven what-if simulator, not a cluster - it replays a five-month ByteDance trace on a single machine. Available + Functional. Concern: it is a measurement study, so the improvement angle is a better simulator/attribution method rather than a performance win; check that the released trace subset is large enough to support new claims. |
| `osdi26-cocoon-a-system-architecture-for-differe` | Cocoon: A System Architecture for Differentially Private Training with Correlated Noises | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/kim-donghwan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-kim-donghwan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/psu-paws/Cocoon) | Single-node DP-training framework; the artifact appendix says main experiments ran on **RTX A5000 24 GB** GPUs - the same GPU as this machine. Concern: their box has 4 A5000s and two sockets, so runs must be scaled to one GPU; some results are embedding-table-heavy |
| `osdi26-grace-unlocking-cuda-graphs-with-compile` | GraCE: Unlocking CUDA Graphs with Compiler Support for ML Workloads | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/ghosh) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-ghosh.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/csl-iisc/GraCE-OSDI26-Artifact) | CPU-launch-overhead elimination for short ML kernels via CUDA Graphs - single GPU, TorchBench/HuggingFace/TIMM workloads, and launch overhead is *more* visible on a modest GPU. Concerns: the prebuilt artifact is a Docker image built for CUDA 12.8 (no Docker here, and driver 535 supports CUDA 12.2 natively) - needs a source build in a conda env |
| `osdi26-rlinf-flexible-and-efficient-large-scale` | RLinf: Flexible and Efficient Large-Scale Reinforcement Learning via Macro-to-Micro Flow Transformation | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/yu-chao) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-yu-chao.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/RLinf/RLinf) | The most reusable of the five RL-training papers: a fully open, actively maintained RL framework whose M2Flow placement/scheduling abstraction can be exercised on a single node. Concerns: headline results are many-GPU; on 24 GB only small policies (~1.5B, LoRA) are realistic, and the collocate-vs-disaggregate comparison largely disappears with one GPU |
| `sigmetrics24-netdiffusion-network-data-augmentation-t` | NetDiffusion: Network Data Augmentation Through Protocol-Constrained Traffic Generation | SIGMETRICS 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3639037) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.08543) | [repo](https://github.com/noise-lab/NetDiffusion) | A user-space generative data pipeline: a fine-tuned Stable Diffusion plus ControlNet model with protocol-constraint post-processing, sized for a single consumer GPU, so a 24 GB A5000 is sufficient for both fine-tuning (LoRA) and inference; the repo is popular and maintained, and generation cost per trace is the paper's acknowledged weak point, which makes a performance-improvement hypothesis natural. Concern: topic fit is the weakest in this list - it is generative ML applied to networking data rather than a core systems artifact, so the audit may prefer to drop it. Concern: the paper's headline results are Google-fleet-scale; only a single-node, multi-process scale-down is possible, and the released code is a production subsystem rather than a paper artifact. |

Repo description matches the paper and was last pushed 2025-11-21, the SoCC presentation date. Concerns - repository authorship was inferred from the description and timing rather than from a link in the paper; the paper assumes a small multi-GPU edge cluster, so a single-GPU scale-down is needed. |

Concern - originally evaluated on a multi-device edge testbed; here it must be emulated with cgroup-v2-limited processes on one host (cgroup cpu/memory controllers are delegated, so this is possible). |

Single GPU, fully user space. Concerns - this is an 8-page measurement study rather than a system, so an improvement project would have to extend the methodology or build a policy on top of it; and some GPU counter collection may require admin-enabled profiling, which is unavailable here. |
| `sosp23-bagpipe-accelerating-deep-recommendation` | Bagpipe - Accelerating Deep Recommendation Model Training | SOSP 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3600006.3613142) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.12429) | [repo](https://github.com/iidsample/bagpipe_artifacts) | The core mechanism is a lookahead oracle cache that prefetches hot embeddings ahead of the training loop, which is meaningful with one trainer plus one cache process on a single node with one GPU. Kept as borderline per the scope rule. Concerns - serious machine risk: the released AE artifact is an EC2 cluster launcher (ec2_launcher_six_models.py) that provisions a multi-node AWS cluster and the torchrec baseline is distributed, so no single-node scale-down is provided and the team would have to build one; the audit stage should decide whether that is 10-week feasible |

Concerns - the AE README asks for 8x V100-32GB or 1x A100-80GB and 200-700 GB disk, so models must be scaled down to fit 24 GB and 257 GB; reproduction is shipped as a Docker image that has to be ported to conda; no FP8 paths involved so Ampere is otherwise fine. Merge note: scout linked the artifact branch https://github.com/microsoft/SparTA/tree/pit_artifact . |
| `sosp23-gsampler-general-and-efficient-gpu-based` | gSampler - General and Efficient GPU-based Graph Sampling for Graph Learning | SOSP 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3600006.3613168) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3600006.3613168) | [repo](https://github.com/gsampler9/gSampler) | Single-GPU matrix-centric graph sampler with a data-flow-graph optimizer; AE reference hardware is a single V100 so a 24 GB A5000 is comfortable, and everything is user-space PyTorch/DGL/CUDA. Concerns - the AE instructions ask for 256 GB RAM to preprocess ogbn-papers100M and Friendster in CSC format and we only have ~111 GB available, so the study must stay on ogbn-products and LiveJournal or use out-of-core preprocessing |
| `sosp24-apparate-rethinking-early-exits-to-tame` | Apparate: Rethinking Early Exits to Tame Latency-Throughput Tensions in ML Serving | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695963) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.05385) | [repo](https://github.com/dywsjtu/apparate) | Early-exit ramps bolted onto existing serving systems; a pure user-space PyTorch/serving-runtime artifact. Models used (ResNet/BERT/GPT-2 class) fit one 24 GB A5000. Concern: paper evaluates on multi-GPU serving clusters, so a scale-down to one GPU plus a replay of their request traces is needed; goodput numbers will not be directly comparable. |
| `sosp24-improving-dnn-inference-throughput-using` | Improving DNN Inference Throughput Using Practical, Per-Input Compute Adaptation | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695978) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3694715.3695978) | — | E3 splits and replicates blocks of early-exit models to keep batch size constant; a user-space serving-layer mechanism that can be exercised with one A5000 on BERT/GPT-2 class models. Concern: no artifact found - not in the SOSP 2024 AE results, and no repo on the authors' GitHub accounts (checked dywsjtu, MingyuGuan, ruipan, swapnilgandhi, anandpiyer) or the Georgia Tech NEXS project page. Marked unknown rather than out-no-code because the closely related Apparate artifact from the same group is public and E3 code may live unlinked. |
| `sosp24-reducing-energy-bloat-in-large-model-tra` | Reducing Energy Bloat in Large Model Training | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695970) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.06902) | [repo](https://github.com/ml-energy/zeus) | Perseus's core is an offline time-energy Pareto planner over pipeline-stage frequency assignments - a graph algorithm that runs on profiles, so it can be studied and improved without the training cluster. Energy/power readings come from NVML via nvidia-smi, which needs no root and no perf counters. Concern: end-to-end validation in the paper uses multi-GPU pipeline-parallel training; on one A5000 only the planner plus single-GPU DVFS profiling is reproducible, and GPU frequency locking may require privileges. Merge note: scout linked the branch https://github.com/ml-energy/zeus/tree/kronos . |
| `sosp24-silvanforge-a-schedule-guided-retargetab` | SilvanForge: A Schedule-Guided Retargetable Compiler for Decision Tree Inference | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695958) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3694715.3695958) | [repo](https://github.com/asprasad/treebeard) | An ML compiler (MLIR/LLVM-based) that autoschedules decision-tree inference for both CPU and a single GPU - the paper's own evaluation platform is one multicore CPU plus one NVIDIA GPU, matching this machine almost exactly. Concern: needs a user-space LLVM/MLIR toolchain built from source (clang is not installed), which is a real but tractable setup cost. Merge note: scout linked the branch https://github.com/asprasad/treebeard/tree/silvanforge . |
| `sosp24-uncovering-nested-data-parallelism-and-d` | Uncovering Nested Data Parallelism and Data Reuse in DNN Computation with FractalTensor | SOSP 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3694715.3695961) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3694715.3695961) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/FractalTensor) | DNN compiler/IR exploiting nested data parallelism; kernels are generated for a single NVIDIA GPU and the artifact builds against CUDA plus cuDNN in user space. Ampere (sm_86) is supported by its CUTLASS/TileFusion-style tiling. Concerns: the AE badge points at https://github.com/microsoft/nnfusion while the maintained code lives in microsoft/FractalTensor and is described as "under active refactoring"; some generated kernels may assume newer CUDA than the 11.8 system nvcc, so a conda CUDA toolkit would be needed. |
| `sosp25-sailor-automating-distributed-training-o` | Sailor: Automating Distributed Training over Dynamic, Heterogeneous, and Geo-distributed Clusters | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764839) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.17096) | [repo](https://github.com/eth-easl/sailor) | The improvable core - the search-space exploration algorithm and the runtime/memory-footprint simulator - runs entirely on one CPU machine, and simulator accuracy is itself a testable hypothesis. Concerns - the end-to-end throughput and cost claims need heterogeneous multi-node GPU clusters that do not exist here, so only the planner/simulator half is reproducible; the team must scope the project to that half up front. |
| `sosp25-tempo-compiled-dynamic-deep-learning-wit` | Tempo: Compiled Dynamic Deep Learning with Symbolic Dependence Graphs | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764840) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.05408) | [repo](https://github.com/LSDS/Tempo) | Single-node DL compiler; the headline numbers (7x over JAX on Llama-3.2-3B decoding, 54x on RL algorithms with 16x lower peak memory) are all measured on one GPU and a 3B model fits comfortably in 24 GB. Compiler passes (tiling, fusion, polyhedral scheduling) are a rich improvement surface. Concerns - the polyhedral/codegen toolchain may need recent LLVM and CUDA installed per-user via conda. |
| `sosp25-trainverify-equivalence-based-verificati` | TrainVerify: Equivalence-Based Verification for Distributed LLM Training | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764850) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.15961) | [repo](https://github.com/verify-llm/TrainVerify) | Although the subject is distributed training, the artifact itself is symbolic/SMT equivalence checking that runs on CPU only - no GPUs and no cluster are needed to reproduce the Llama3-405B and DeepSeek-V3-671B plan verifications. Shape reduction and stage-wise verification are well-defined targets for a "verify faster" hypothesis. Concerns - verifying frontier-scale plans may be memory and time hungry even on 125 GB; success is measured in verification time and coverage, not system throughput. |

Emulates thousand-GPU LLM training by running only selected ranks for real and replaying the rest as virtual participants - that hybrid emulation is exactly a single-GPU scale-down. Concern: fidelity claims are validated against real production clusters we cannot access and it builds on CRIU/cuda-checkpoint which may want privileges. No artifact link in the preprint. |

Title indicates decoupling GPU memory to survive shallow (transient) GPU disruptions - a GPU memory-management mechanism that is plausible to demo on one A5000. Concern: no abstract or preprint is public yet so the scale of the evaluation is unknown. Author group page lists it but the Paper link is not live. |
| `sosp26-ave-guiding-agentic-gpu-optimization-usi` | Ave: Guiding Agentic GPU Optimization Using Data-Flow Invariants | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18616) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18616) | — | ARGUS - agentic generation of GPU kernels guided by compile-time data-flow invariants, with a tile-based Python DSL. Kernel generation and benchmarking work on a single GPU. Concerns: the DSL exposes modern tensor-core or TMA style instructions that may assume Hopper rather than Ampere sm_86, and reproducing the agent loop costs LLM API calls. No artifact URL in the preprint. |

## caching (51)

| id | title | venue | paper | pdf | repo | note |

| `asplos23-evstore-storage-and-caching-capabilities` | EVStore: Storage and Caching Capabilities for Scaling Embedding Tables in Deep Recommendation Systems | ASPLOS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3575693.3575718) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3575693.3575718) | [repo](https://github.com/ucare-uchicago/ev-store-dlrm) | Multi-layer embedding-table cache for DLRM inference - single node CPU/GPU user space  cache admission/eviction policy is a clean improvement target |
| `asplos25-gigaflow-pipeline-aware-sub-traversal-ca` | Gigaflow: Pipeline-Aware Sub-Traversal Caching for Modern SmartNICs | ASPLOS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3676641.3716000) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3676641.3716000) | [repo](https://github.com/gigaflow-vswitch/gvs) | Pipeline-aware sub-traversal flow-cache design for vSwitches - the caching algorithm and its trace-driven evaluation are software; concern: the headline deployment is on a SmartNIC, so only the software vSwitch/simulation path is reproducible here |
| `atc23-adaptive-online-cache-capacity-optimizat` | Adaptive Online Cache Capacity Optimization via Lightweight Working Set Size Estimation at Scale (Cuki) | ATC 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc23/presentation/gu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc23-gu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/shadowcache/Cuki-Artifact-WSS-Estimation) | Core artifact is a sketch-like data structure for online working-set-size / item-repetition-ratio / miss-ratio-curve estimation, plus an adaptive cache-capacity tuner - trace-driven cache work, exactly the caching topic, and AE Available+Functional+Reproduced. Concerns: the end-to-end numbers use Alluxio plus Presto (extra repos Cuki-Artifact-Alluxio, Cuki-Artifact-Presto) which are JVM-heavy and normally multi-node; the standalone estimator is trivially runnable on traces, which is the realistic project scope. |
| `atc24-data-caching-for-enterprise-grade-petaby` | Data Caching for Enterprise-Grade Petabyte-Scale OLAP | ATC 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc24/presentation/tang) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc24-tang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Alluxio/alluxio) | Local SSD cache for petabyte-scale OLAP - admission, eviction, quota and page-affinity policies are exactly the caching topic and can be studied trace-driven on one NVMe disk. Concern: the paper is a deployed-systems experience report with no dedicated artifact; the implementation it describes is the local cache inside Alluxio open source, so the mapping from paper to code has to be verified |
| `eurosys23-frozenhot-cache-rethinking-cache-managem` | FrozenHot Cache: Rethinking Cache Management for Modern Hardware | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3587446) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3587446) | [repo](https://github.com/ziyueqiu/FrozenHot) | In-memory cache eviction/promotion scalability - pure user-space C++ library plus a trace-driven benchmark harness. Concern - the repo README asks for at least 32 physical cores per socket and around 100 GiB per NUMA node; this machine has 16 cores / 32 threads and 111 GiB free on one NUMA node so absolute numbers will be lower, and cache traces must be downloaded. |
| `eurosys24-flows-balanced-mrc-profiling-for-heterog` | FLOWS: Balanced MRC Profiling for Heterogeneous Object-Size Cache | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650078) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650078) | [repo](https://github.com/JasonGuo98/FLOWS-Balanced-MRC-Profiling-for-Heterogeneous-Object-Size-Cache) | Spatial-sampling MRC profiler that fixes byte-miss-ratio error for variable-object-size (CDN) traces. Trace-driven, user-space, no hardware requirements. Pairs naturally with TTLs Matter as a two-paper comparison target |
| `eurosys24-ttls-matter-efficient-cache-sizing-with` | TTLs Matter: Efficient Cache Sizing with TTL-Aware Miss Ratio Curves and Working Set Sizes | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650066) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650066) | [repo](https://github.com/SariSultan/TTLsMatter-EuroSys24) | Purely trace-driven MRC/WSS algorithms (TTL-aware SHARDS, TTL-aware HyperLogLog) - CPU and RAM only, no privileged access. 28 public cache traces with 113 billion accesses; disk space for the full trace set (~257 GB free) is the only real constraint |
| `eurosys26-2dio-configurable-and-cache-accurate-tra` | 2DIO: Configurable and Cache-Accurate Trace Generation for Storage Benchmarking | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3769391) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.19971) | [repo](https://github.com/Effygal/trace-gen) | Cache-accurate synthetic I/O trace generator with tunable hit-ratio curves - README shows a user-space C++ and Python build with no root requirement. Excellent fit for cache policy work |
| `eurosys26-a-logically-disaggregated-cache-for-repl` | A Logically Disaggregated Cache for Replicated Storage Systems | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3803608) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3803608) | [repo](https://github.com/dassl-uiuc/Logically-Disaggregated-Caches) | Disaggregating the embedded caches of storage replicas into one logical cache - artifact implements LDC in a key-value store, so replicas can be run as several processes here. Concern: cross-replica network effects are lost on a single host |
| `fast24-kosmo-efficient-online-miss-ratio-curve` | Kosmo: Efficient Online Miss Ratio Curve Generation for Eviction Policy Evaluation | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/shakiba) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-shakiba.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Stumm-Lab/kosmo-fast24) | Pure trace-driven MRC generation for non-inclusive eviction policies (LFU, FIFO, 2Q, LRFU). Rust binaries, public cache traces (Twitter cache-trace, 52 traces), no privileged access and no special hardware. Artifact also archived at Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.10569925. Only concern is trace download size against the ~257 GB free on /home, which is manageable by subsetting traces. |
| `fast24-symbiosis-the-art-of-application-and-ker` | Symbiosis: The Art of Application and Kernel Cache Cooperation | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/dai) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-dai.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/daiyifandanny/Symbiosis) | Dynamically splits memory between an application cache and the kernel page cache for LevelDB, RocksDB and WiredTiger. Entirely user-space: modified KV stores plus a Python/simpy offline simulator, and memory budgets are enforced with cgroup v2, which env.md delegates to the user slice. No root, no kernel changes, single node. Strong improvement target - the sizing policy is a small, self-contained algorithm. |
| `fast25-3l-cache-low-overhead-and-precise-learni` | 3L-Cache: Low Overhead and Precise Learning-based Eviction Policy for Caches | FAST 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast25/presentation/zhou-wenbin) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast25-zhou-wenbin.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/optiq-lab/3L-Cache) | Learning-based cache eviction policy evaluated entirely by trace-driven simulation on top of libCacheSim - user-space C++, CPU only, no root, and the headline metric (CPU overhead vs miss ratio) is exactly the kind of trade-off a 10-week improvement project can attack. Concerns - the paper's 4855-trace corpus is many TB; must subset to a few trace families and argue representativeness. |
| `fast26-cache-centric-multi-resource-allocation` | Cache-Centric Multi-Resource Allocation for Storage Services | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/ye) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-ye.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/WiscADSL/hare) | HARE is a cache-size-aware multi-resource (cache/IO/CPU) allocation algorithm - a policy problem that is naturally simulated or run in user space, with public Twitter cache traces. Two prototypes also released: https://github.com/WiscADSL/hopperkv and https://github.com/WiscADSL/bunnyfs. Concerns: BunnyFS issues I/O through SPDK, whose setup (hugepages, VFIO binding) normally needs root; HopperKV caches DynamoDB behind Redis and would need DynamoDB-local or an AWS account. The algorithm itself is the tractable target. |
| `fast26-how-soon-is-now-preloading-images-for-vi` | How Soon is Now? Preloading Images for Virtual Disks with ThinkAhead | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/chen) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-chen.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/Master-Chen-Xin-Qi/FAST26_AE) | Data-driven block preloading (prefetch/admission policy) for virtual disk images, evaluated largely by trace-driven simulation; production Alibaba EBS image-boot traces released at https://tianchi.aliyun.com/dataset/216164. Concern: the paper also reports cluster experiments on Alibaba EBS, which cannot be reproduced - only the prediction + simulation half is in reach here. |
| `middleware25-leveraging-approximate-caching-for-faste` | Leveraging Approximate Caching for Faster Retrieval-Augmented Generation | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770776) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.05530) | [repo](https://github.com/sacs-epfl/proximity) | Proximity is an LSH-based approximate key-value cache in front of a RAG vector database - squarely a cache admission/eviction/approximation policy study, and the clearest single-node fit in the venue. Rust implementation, MMLU and MedRAG benchmarks, embedding model and a 7-8B generator both fit in 24 GB FP16. Rust is not installed but rustup into $HOME needs no root. Concern: the MedRAG corpus plus index is a multi-tens-of-GB download against ~257 GB free. |
| `mlsys23-communication-efficient-graph-neural-net` | Communication-Efficient Graph Neural Networks with Probabilistic Neighborhood Expansion Analysis and Caching | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/9b09f3893ff30373b682bde69082c75b-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/9b09f3893ff30373b682bde69082c75b-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/MITIBMxGraph/SALIENT_plusplus) | VIP-based caching policy that decides which remote vertex features to cache and which to keep in GPU memory for minibatch GNN training - the caching analysis and the GPU feature cache are meaningful on one GPU with a partitioned graph in host memory - concern - the published speedups come from a multi-machine setting so cross-partition transfers must be emulated between processes |
| `mlsys25-marconi-prefix-caching-for-the-era-of-hy` | Marconi: Prefix Caching for the Era of Hybrid LLMs | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/7c180af017258d239bac6248d1eb26ac-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/7c180af017258d239bac6248d1eb26ac-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ruipeterpan/marconi) | Prefix-cache admission and eviction policy for hybrid LLMs - the artifact is a trace-driven Python simulator so the whole evaluation runs on CPU, with the GPU only needed for optional end-to-end checks - ideal project target |
| `mlsys26-agenticcache-cache-driven-asynchronous-p` | AgenticCache: Cache-Driven Asynchronous Planning for Embodied AI Agents | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/c66a9db149261435664284a20b6f1d42-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/c66a9db149261435664284a20b6f1d42-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/hojoonleokim/MLSys26_AgenticCache) | Plan-transition cache with an asynchronous background updater for embodied agents - a genuine cache admission/eviction/validation policy question, evaluated in user-space simulators with released code. Concern: needs an LLM backend, either a local 7-8B model or API budget |
| `nsdi24-sieve-is-simpler-than-lru-an-efficient-t` | SIEVE is Simpler than LRU: an Efficient Turn-Key Eviction Algorithm for Web Caches | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/zhang-yazhuo) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-zhang-yazhuo.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/cacheMon/NSDI24-SIEVE) | Cache eviction algorithm evaluated on 1559 open traces with a CPU-only user-space simulator (libCacheSim) plus patches to five production cache libraries; no root, no GPU, no network. Strongest fit of this venue-year. Concerns: the full trace corpus is large relative to 257 GB free disk so a subset is needed; throughput/scalability claims were made with 16 threads which matches this machine. |
| `nsdi26-cachecatalyst-enhancing-web-caching-for` | CacheCatalyst: Enhancing Web Caching for the Latency-Constrained Internet | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/hosseini) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-hosseini.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/toorin-lab/CacheCatalyst) | Web cache revalidation policy - early validation during page load; entirely user-space (browser plus Caddy origin) and trace or emulation driven on one host. Concern - realistic latency effects need network emulation which is available only without root-level tc in some setups. |
| `nsdi26-latency-aware-caching-with-delayed-hits` | Latency-Aware Caching with Delayed Hits: From Bursty Traffic to Pipeline Architectures | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/keren) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-keren.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/NadavKeren/Adaptive-Pipeline-Cache) | Pipeline of orthogonal cache policies with adaptive memory split, plus latency-aware handling of delayed hits - trace-driven simulation on top of the Caffeine framework, purely CPU and memory bound. Concern - Java build and large public cache traces need disk, both fine here. |
| `osdi23-take-out-the-trache-maximizing-tra-nsact` | Take Out the TraChe: Maximizing (Tra)nsactional Ca(che) Hit Rate | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/cheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-cheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/audreyccheng/detox) | Squarely on-topic: a new cache eviction and prefetching policy (DeToX) driven by transactional dependencies, evaluated with OLTP traces and benchmarks on a single node in user space. Strong fit for a policy-improvement hypothesis with trace-driven replay. |
| `osdi24-fairywren-a-sustainable-cache-for-emergi` | FairyWREN: A Sustainable Cache for Emerging Write-Read-Erase Flash Interfaces | OSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/mcallister) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-mcallister.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/saramcallister/CacheLib-fairywren) | Flash cache that co-designs admission/eviction with garbage collection - a CacheLib fork by the first author is public although the paper itself gives no code link. Concern - the WREN/FDP interface needs a capable SSD and the machine has no root for device configuration so the study would have to be simulation or emulation based on the released carbon and write-amplification models |
| `sigmetrics23-optimistic-no-regret-algorithms-for-disc` | Optimistic No-regret Algorithms for Discrete Caching | SIGMETRICS 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3570608) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2208.06414) | [repo](https://github.com/Naram-m/discrete-online-learning) | Prediction-assisted (oracle/NN-guided) discrete caching policies with a new optimistic Follow-the-Perturbed-Leader family; evaluated trace-driven on MovieLens and similar request traces. Pure CPU Python, no root, no GPU needed - a natural fit for the machine. Improvement angle - the policies are O(N) per request in the number of files and the paper itself trades performance against complexity; a faster implementation or a real cache integration is a testable hypothesis. Concern - the paper is theory-heavy and the released code is a numerical-experiment harness rather than a cache system so a reproduction may feel thin without extending it to a real workload trace |
| `sigmetrics24-shrinking-vod-traffic-via-renyi-entropic` | Shrinking VOD Traffic via Renyi-Entropic Optimal Transport | SIGMETRICS 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3639033) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3639033) | — | Cache-efficiency work: it proposes Renyi entropy as a proxy for the cache footprint of a video catalogue, proves that minimizing it maximizes soft cache hit ratio, and evaluates on a real VOD request trace. Entirely CPU-side optimal-transport computation over a request trace, so the machine is not a constraint. Concern: no official implementation found - I searched the GitHub repository API for the system name and for "optimal transport / soft cache hit ratio" phrasings, checked the ACM DL landing page and the Edinburgh and Surrey institutional repository records, and found no code or Zenodo artifact link; the Edinburgh open-access PDF is behind a Cloudflare challenge from this host, so I could not read the first-page footnote and cannot rule out a link there. Listed as repo unknown per the code-check rule rather than out-no-code. |
| `sigmetrics25-scaleopt-a-scalable-optimal-page-replace` | ScaleOPT: A Scalable Optimal Page Replacement Policy Simulator | SIGMETRICS 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3700426) | — | [repo](https://github.com/syslab-CAU/ScaleOPT) | Parallel trace-driven simulator for Belady/OPT page replacement; plain C, CPU-only, compares directly against libCacheSim and webcachesim. Runs entirely in user space on cache traces. Concerns: evaluated on a 72-core machine, so our 16-core/32-thread CPU is a meaningful scale-down (speedup ceiling will be lower); repo has had no pushes since Oct 2024 and the ACM paper is closed-access so the trace list must be recovered from the repo. |
| `sigmetrics25-using-lock-free-design-for-throughput-op` | Using Lock-Free Design for Throughput-Optimized Cache Eviction (Mobius) | SIGMETRICS 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3727136) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3727136) | [repo](https://github.com/Anonymous-chaos/Cachelib-with-Mobius) | Lock-free FIFO-queue cache eviction policy (SIEVE-like) evaluated for concurrent throughput and hit ratio; pure user-space C++ integrated into CacheLib and RocksDB. Ideal fit for a 16-core/32-thread box. Second official repo is RocksDB-with-Mobius under the same account. Concern: the artifact account is an anonymized handle with no README-level build docs visible; scaling claims were made on a higher-core-count cluster so absolute numbers will differ. |
| `sigmetrics26-performance-analysis-of-the-randomized-s` | Performance Analysis of the Randomized SIEVE/CLOCK cache replacement algorithm | SIGMETRICS 2026 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3805647) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3805647) | — | Randomized SIEVE/CLOCK with multiple access bits plus a mean-field model and trace simulations - textbook fit for a cache-policy improvement project and needs only CPU. Concern: no public repository found. I searched GitHub repositories for SIEVE CLOCK cache and the authors, checked Van Houdt's and Desnoyers' pages, and could not reach the ACM DL full text, so artifact status is genuinely unknown rather than confirmed absent |

| `fast23-adoc-automatically-harmonizing-dataflow` | ADOC: Automatically Harmonizing Dataflow Between Components in Log-Structured Key-Value Stores for Improved Performance | FAST 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast23/presentation/yu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast23-yu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/supermt/FEAT_7.11) | Excellent fit. Pure user-space RocksDB auto-tuning framework that controls write stalls by adjusting thread count and batch size to limit data overflow. Evaluation is single-node RocksDB on NVMe SSDs - exactly the target machine. 16 cores and local NVMe are enough to reproduce the write-stall regimes. Concern: the paper sweeps several device classes (SATA SSD, NVMe SSD, Optane) and we only have NVMe, so device-sensitivity results would have to be narrowed or emulated with cgroup v2 I/O throttling. |
| `fast23-pararc-embracing-sub-packetization-for-r` | ParaRC: Embracing Sub-Packetization for Repair Parallelization in MSR-Coded Storage | FAST 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast23/presentation/li-xiaolu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast23-li.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ukulililixl/ParaRC) | The core contribution is an algorithmic one - a heuristic that trades repair bandwidth against maximum per-node repair load in MSR codes - and the bandwidth/load trade-off curve can be studied purely by simulation on one machine. Concern: the released prototype is a distributed repair system whose end-to-end numbers come from a ~10-node cluster over a real network. On this machine it would have to run as multiple processes on one host, so absolute degraded-read and full-node-recovery times will be distorted; the defensible experiments are the load-balancing heuristic and simulated repair schedules. |
| `fast24-elect-enabling-erasure-coding-tiering-fo` | ELECT: Enabling Erasure Coding Tiering for LSM-tree-based Storage | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/ren) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-ren.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/adslabcuhk/elect) | Hybrid replication/erasure-coding tiering for an LSM-tree KV store, implemented on Cassandra. Borderline on the machine: the artifact appendix recommends at least eight networked machines (six for the EC stripe). Co-locating 6-8 Cassandra JVMs as processes on this single host is possible within 125 GiB and is the standard CCM-style setup, and the headline storage-savings claim is not latency-sensitive; end-to-end throughput/latency numbers would not transfer. Needs Java in user space (not installed, but conda-installable) and Intel ISA-L, which builds from source. Audit stage should judge whether the single-host scale-down is acceptable. |
| `fast24-ionia-high-performance-replication-for-m` | IONIA: High-Performance Replication for Modern Disk-based KV Stores | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/xu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-xu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/dassl-uiuc/Ionia) | Replication protocol exploiting LSM/write-optimized KV-store interface and performance characteristics to get 1-RTT writes and any-replica reads. Concern: it is a distributed protocol normally run on a small cluster. env.md permits emulating distributed behaviour with multiple processes on this host, and 3-5 replica processes over loopback fit easily in 16 cores / 125 GiB; RTT-sensitive latency numbers would be distorted, so a reproduction would need tc-free latency emulation or a focus on the storage-side (background execution, read scalability) claims. No RDMA or special NIC required. |
| `fast24-midas-minimizing-write-amplification-in` | MIDAS: Minimizing Write Amplification in Log-Structured Systems through Adaptive Group Number and Size Configuration | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/oh) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-oh.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/postech-caoslab/MiDAS) | Analytical (UID + Markov-chain) model that adapts group count and size to minimize GC write amplification in log-structured systems. Main results come from a user-space simulator, separately released at https://github.com/sungkyun123/MiDAS-Simulation and archived at Zenodo 10409599 - that part runs anywhere. Concern: the hardware prototype needs a Xilinx VCU108 FPGA with custom NAND modules that the authors state are not publicly available, so only the simulation-based claims are reproducible here. That is still a complete improvement target (the WAF/throughput comparisons against state-of-the-art GC are simulator results). |
| `fast24-metis-file-system-model-checking-via-ver` | Metis: File System Model Checking via Versatile Input and State Exploration | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/liu-yifei) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-liu-yifei.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/sbu-fsl/Metis) | Model-checking framework plus RefFS, a FUSE reference file system (https://github.com/sbu-fsl/RefFS). Concern: the headline experiments check in-kernel file systems (ext4, XFS, BtrFS) on brd RAM block devices, which needs root for modprobe and mount/unmount - not available here. A scaled-down study over FUSE-only targets (RefFS vs fuse-ext2) is feasible since /dev/fuse is world-rw and fusermount3 is setuid. Memory-hungry (search width x mountable size) but 125 GiB RAM helps. Audit stage should confirm the FUSE-only path is a viable project. |
| `fast24-physical-vs-logical-indexing-with-idea-i` | Physical vs. Logical Indexing with IDEA: Inverted Deduplication-Aware Index | FAST 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast24/presentation/levi) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast24-levi.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/asaflevi0812/IDEA) | Deduplication-aware inverted term index built on Lucene and the Destor deduplication prototype. Single server, user-space Java/C++, HDD or SSD backing store - their own setup was one 128 GB Xeon box, comfortably below this machine. Concern: the artifact targets Ubuntu 16.04 (old Destor dependencies), so building on Debian 12 may need a conda/source toolchain; no system packages are strictly required. |
| `fast25-liquid-state-drive-a-case-for-dna-block` | Liquid-State Drive: A Case for DNA Block Device for Enormous Data | FAST 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast25/presentation/zhou-jiahao) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast25-zhou-jiahao.pdf) | [repo](https://ipads.se.sjtu.edu.cn/projects/liqsd) | Block-device design (dual-layer translation table, symbiotic metadata, delayed invalidation) evaluated entirely on a user-space DNA-storage simulator with a small SSD as the metadata tier - no exotic hardware is needed to run it, and the mapping/GC policies are improvable. Concerns - the paper says only the simulator is released, and the SJTU IPADS project host did not respond from this network at check time, so the repo could not be verified; GitHub search for "LiqSD" found nothing. Treat the repo link as unconfirmed. |
| `fast25-on-scalable-integrity-checking-for-secur` | On Scalable Integrity Checking for Secure Cloud Disks | FAST 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast25/presentation/burke) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast25-burke.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/MadSP-McDaniel/dmt) | Dynamic Merkle Trees are a 5 KLoC user-space C++ data structure on the storage I/O path, driven by synthetic Zipfian and real Alibaba block traces - the algorithmic contribution is reproducible without privileges. Concerns - the paper's block-device integration uses BDUS, which ships an out-of-tree kernel module that cannot be loaded without root; the experiment harness would have to be re-hosted on FUSE or on a pure user-space trace replayer, which changes the absolute numbers. |
| `fast25-vectorcdc-accelerating-data-deduplicatio` | VectorCDC: Accelerating Data Deduplication with Vector Instructions | FAST 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast25/presentation/udayashankar) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast25-udayashankar.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/UWASL/dedup-bench) | Content-defined chunking accelerated with SSE/AVX - pure user-space C++ benchmark tool (DedupBench), no root, no special hardware, builds with plain gcc. Concerns - Zen 3 Threadripper has SSE-128 and AVX-256 but no AVX-512, so the AVX-512 numbers cannot be reproduced; only hashless CDC variants (AE, MAXP, RAM) are vectorized, and the DEB dataset lives on Kaggle (download size to check). |
| `fast26-range-as-a-key-is-the-key-fast-and-compa` | "Range as a Key" is the Key! Fast and Compact Cloud Block Store Index with RASK | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/zhao) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-zhao.pdf) | [repo](https://ipads.se.sjtu.edu.cn:1312/opensource/rask-index) | User-space in-memory range index for block stores, evaluated trace-driven against ten baseline indexes - ideal single-machine, no-root study. Alibaba EBS traces released at https://tianchi.aliyun.com/dataset/218875. Concern: the SJTU-hosted GitLab (ipads.se.sjtu.edu.cn) did not respond from this network when checked; availability should be re-verified at audit time. |
| `fast26-holistic-and-automated-task-scheduling-f` | Holistic and Automated Task Scheduling for Distributed LSM-tree-based Storage | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/ren) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-ren.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/adslabcuhk/hats) | HATS co-schedules foreground reads and background compaction in Cassandra; Java user-space prototype, no kernel or special hardware. Concern: it is a distributed KV store - a cluster must be emulated as several Cassandra JVMs on this one host (16 cores / 125 GiB is enough for a 3-5 node ring), and replica-selection results will be less faithful than on real nodes. |
| `fast26-less-is-more-for-i-o-efficient-repairs-i` | LESS is More for I/O-Efficient Repairs in Erasure-Coded Storage | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/cheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-cheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/adslabcuhk/less) | Repair-friendly erasure code construction reducing both bytes read and I/O seeks; the coding core is a user-space library (ISA-L based) and the trade-off can be studied with local microbenchmarks. Concern: end-to-end numbers come from HDFS on a local cluster - here that means a pseudo-distributed / multi-process HDFS on one host, which changes the network/seek balance. |
| `fast26-odinann-direct-insert-for-consistently-s` | OdinANN: Direct Insert for Consistently Stable Performance in Billion-Scale Graph-Based Vector Search | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/guo) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-guo.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/thustorage/PipeANN) | On-disk graph ANNS index with direct insert; entirely user space, single node, CPU + NVMe. Code is shipped inside the authors' PipeANN repo (README cites the FAST '26 OdinANN paper and notes PipeANN is integrated into OdinANN). Concerns: paper machine has 512 GB RAM and billion-scale datasets; with 125 GiB RAM and ~257 GB free disk the study must scale down to 10M-100M vectors (SIFT100M-class), which the artifact supports. |
| `fast26-parasync-exploiting-fine-grained-paralle` | ParaSync: Exploiting Fine-Grained Parallelism for Efficient File Synchronization | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/zhang-zhihao-parasync) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-zhang-zhihao-parasync.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/nicexlab/parasync) | Parallel content-defined chunking + streaming chunk matching + pipelined reconstruction; user-space C++, CPU-bound, benefits from the 16-core/32-thread CPU. Same WAN-emulation caveat as SkySync; chunking speedup (7.6x claimed) is measurable locally with no privileges. |
| `fast26-skysync-accelerating-file-synchronizatio` | SkySync: Accelerating File Synchronization with Collaborative Delta Generation | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/zhang-zhihao) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-zhang-zhihao.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/skysync-project/skysync) | Reuses storage-layer checksums to cut delta-generation cost; implemented by extending rsync and dsync protocols, fully user space. Client and server can both run on this host. Concern: the WAN numbers need link emulation, and tc/netem requires root - LAN/loopback and CPU-cost measurements are still fully reproducible. |
| `middleware23-glider-serverless-ephemeral-stateful-nea` | Glider: Serverless Ephemeral Stateful Near-Data Computation | MIDDLEWARE 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3590140.3629119) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3590140.3629119) | [repo](https://github.com/CLOUDLAB-URV/glider-store) | Ephemeral storage for serverless analytics that adds "storage actions" - stateful near-data operators plus an I/O streaming interface - so intermediate data is not shuffled through object storage. Core artifact is a user-space Java storage service (an Apache Crail fork) plus example applications in a second repo (https://github.com/CLOUDLAB-URV/glider-examples); shuffle/aggregation offload placement is a natural improvement target. Concerns - Crail is built for RDMA/NVMf hardware and Glider is normally driven by FaaS functions on a cloud platform; the project would have to stand on Crail's TCP tier and drive it with Lithops' localhost backend or plain processes, which is untested here |
| `middleware23-pravega-a-tiered-storage-system-for-data` | Pravega: A Tiered Storage System for Data Streams | MIDDLEWARE 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3590140.3629113) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3590140.3629113) | [repo](https://github.com/pravega/pravega) | Tiered stream storage - hot append-only tier plus automatic migration to long-term object/file storage, with elastic per-stream segment scaling. Mature, fully open-source Java implementation with a documented single-process standalone mode; the tiering and auto-scaling policies are exactly the kind of user-space mechanism that can be re-designed and measured. Concerns - the paper evaluates a distributed deployment on AWS against Kafka and Pulsar, and the full stack pulls in Zookeeper plus Apache BookKeeper and an S3/NFS tier; a single-node scale-down (all three systems in one host, MinIO or local FS as tier 2) is plausible but needs to be validated early, and Java has to be installed through conda |
| `middleware24-seqcdc-hashless-content-defined-chunking` | SeqCDC: Hashless Content-Defined Chunking for Data Deduplication | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700766) | — | [repo](https://github.com/UWASL/dedup-bench) | Best machine fit in the venue: pure user-space single-threaded CPU chunking measured as MB/s on local files. The official DedupBench tool from the authors' lab lists SeqCDC and cites this exact paper, and the evaluation dataset is published on Kaggle. Concern: only ~257 GB free on /home, so the larger corpora must be subsetted. |
| `middleware24-towards-affordable-reproducibility-using` | Towards Affordable Reproducibility Using Scalable Capture and Comparison of Intermediate Multi-Run Results | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700780) | — | [repo](https://github.com/DataStates/state-diff) | Hash/Merkle-tree comparison of checkpoint data with error bounds; the artifact is a user-space C++/Kokkos library whose README cites this paper by DOI. The hashing, tree construction and I/O-reduction mechanisms are all measurable on one node. Concern: the paper drives the system from HACC cosmology runs on an HPC cluster, so a single-machine study needs scaled-down or synthetic checkpoint streams; build needs Kokkos, io_uring and optionally ADIOS2, all installable in $HOME. |
| `mlsys26-leann-a-low-storage-overhead-vector-inde` | LEANN: A Low-Storage Overhead Vector Index | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/e27ea0cd50b798ff8942caf9203f0992-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/e27ea0cd50b798ff8942caf9203f0992-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/yichuan-w/LEANN) | Storage-efficient vector index that recomputes embeddings on the fly instead of storing them, explicitly targeted at personal devices and single machines. Released code, clear storage/latency/recall trade-off to push on |
| `nsdi26-upfuzz-detecting-data-format-incompatibi` | UpFuzz: Detecting Data Format Incompatibility Bugs during Distributed Storage System Upgrade | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/han) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-han.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/zlab-purdue/upfuzz) | Upgrade-path fuzzing for distributed storage systems (Cassandra, HDFS class) driven by data-format properties of persisted state - runs as local multi-process clusters, CPU and disk only. Concern - each upgrade test is expensive so a 10-week budget limits the campaign length. |
| `nsdi26-zipllm-efficient-llm-storage-via-model-a` | ZipLLM: Efficient LLM Storage via Model-Aware Synergistic Data Deduplication and Compression | NSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi26/presentation/wang-zirui) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-wang-zirui.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ds2-lab/ZipLLM) | Model-aware delta compression (BitX) plus tensor-level deduplication for LLM model-hub storage - CPU and disk only, no GPU needed, and Hugging Face model families are freely downloadable. Excellent fit for the 16-core/125 GiB/257 GB machine. Concern - the full Hugging Face characterization is petabyte scale so the study must be scoped to a few model families. |
| `osdi23-k9db-privacy-compliant-storage-for-web-a` | K9db: Privacy-Compliant Storage For Web Applications By Construction | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/albab) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-albab.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/brownsys/K9db) | MySQL-compatible single-node storage engine layered on RocksDB; user-space build, workloads are single-machine web-app benchmarks. Concern: the headline contribution is GDPR compliance, so a performance-improvement hypothesis has to target its storage organisation and query path rather than the compliance story. |
| `osdi23-lvmt-an-efficient-authenticated-storage` | LVMT: An Efficient Authenticated Storage for Blockchain | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/li-chenxing) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-li-chenxing.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ChenxingLi/authenticated-storage-benchmarks) | Authenticated key-value storage layer (versioned multipoint trie over an LSM store) evaluated on replayed Ethereum traces on one machine. Rust, user-space, no special hardware. Concern: blockchain framing and a cryptographic (vector-commitment) core, and the AE badge link points at the plotting repo so the benchmark harness above is the substantive artifact. |
| `osdi23-vbase-unifying-online-vector-similarity` | VBASE: Unifying Online Vector Similarity Search and Relational Queries via Relaxed Monotonicity | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/zhang-qianxi) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-zhang-qianxi_1.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/MSVBASE) | Single-node query engine embedding vector indices into PostgreSQL; entirely user-space, CPU-bound, datasets fit the 257 GB of free disk. Concern: the released build is normally driven through containers, so the Dockerfile would have to be ported to a conda/source build. AE artifact mirror is https://github.com/Catoverflow/VBASE-artifacts . |
| `osdi24-slope-high-compression-and-fast-search-o` | μSlope: High Compression and Fast Search on Semi-Structured Logs | OSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/wang-rui) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-wang-rui.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/y-scope/clp) | Compression and search engine for semi-structured logs - CPU-only user-space C++ plus a large disk footprint that fits in 257 GB. Concern - full-scale log datasets used in the paper may need to be subsampled |

Badges: Available + Functional + Reproduced. Serious concern: both CapybaraKV and its baselines target Intel Optane persistent memory, which env.md rules out - the performance half of the paper cannot be reproduced without DRAM-backed PM emulation, so a project here would have to be framed around the verification/proof-effort side. |
| `osdi26-arctic-a-practical-lock-free-adaptive-ra` | Arctic: A Practical Lock-Free Adaptive Radix Tree | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/ni) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-ni.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/nwtnni/arctic) | Ideal fit: a lock-free index benchmarked with YCSB and integrated into RocksDB and Turso, all user space, and 16 cores / 32 threads is enough to show scaling. Concern: Rust must be installed in user space (fine via rustup/conda); some scalability claims target higher core counts |
| `sigmetrics23-diffforward-on-balancing-forwarding-traf` | DiffForward: On Balancing Forwarding Traffic for Modern Cloud Block Services via Differentiated Forwarding | SIGMETRICS 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3579444) | — | [repo](https://github.com/wzhzhu/DiffForward) | Separates burst from stable write traffic at the client of a cloud block service and routes burst traffic round-robin into a decentralized log store to remove the proxy-layer imbalance. Official code released by the first author (Python, client/server/manager/utils). In-topic as a storage-engine/IO-path policy that is trace-driven. Concerns - the system is inherently multi-role (client, proxy servers, distributed log store) so on one host it can only be run as co-located processes; no README or build instructions in the repo and no open-access PDF found, so the burst-detection mechanism would have to be reconstructed from the ACM DL version |
| `sigmetrics23-smash-flexible-fast-and-resource-efficie` | Smash: Flexible, Fast, and Resource-efficient Placement and Lookup of Distributed Storage | SIGMETRICS 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3589977) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3589977) | [repo](https://github.com/yliu634/smash) | Object placement and lookup for distributed object stores using a space-efficient lookup structure instead of hashing; claims full placement flexibility with under 60 percent of the DRAM of Ceph/MapX. Core artifact is a self-contained user-space C++ library built with cmake and gcc 12 which the machine has. Concern - the paper's headline evaluation was run in a public cloud with multiple storage nodes, so a single-machine study would have to fall back to multi-process emulation plus data-structure microbenchmarks (memory cost, lookup latency, recovery time); the repo has no CI and was last pushed in 2021, so build friction is a real risk |
| `sigmetrics26-capsule-a-storage-prefetcher-harnessing` | CAPSULE: A Storage Prefetcher Harnessing Spatio-Temporal Locality for Cloud-Scale Workloads | SIGMETRICS 2026 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3788088) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3788088) | [repo](https://github.com/imagesid/capsule) | Clustering-based storage prefetcher evaluated purely by replaying 729 public block traces - MSR, CloudPhysics, Tencent, Alibaba, Meta. | `sigmod23-forestti-a-scalable-inverted-index-orien` | ForestTI: A Scalable Inverted-Index-Oriented Timeseries Management System with Flexible Memory Efficiency | SIGMOD 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3589260) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3589260) | — | In-memory inverted-index structures for time-series ingestion with a tunable memory/throughput knob - storage engine work on one node. Concern: no artifact found |

Included rather than rejected because the abstract could not be retrieved - the ACM DL is unreachable from this host and no preprint or author copy was found, so neither the artifact status nor the exact hardware assumptions could be checked. Concerns - a strongly-consistent object store is likely evaluated on a multi-node cluster and may build on RADOS/Ceph, which would need root; repo is unknown after searching GitHub for LSVD/object-store repos and the authors' pages. Verify before committing to it. |

Concerns - the default storage layer is SPDK, and the README explicitly requires secure boot off and kernel lockdown disabled for user-space PCI/NVMe access, which we cannot do without root; a modified-RocksDB backend is offered as an alternative and would have to be used; billion-scale datasets exceed 257 GB so 10M-100M subsets are needed; build uses gcc-9 and several apt-only libs that must be sourced through conda |
