| `atc25-colocating-ml-inference-and-training-wit` | Colocating ML Inference and Training with Fast GPU Memory Handover | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/wang-jiali) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-wang-jiali.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SiriusInfTra/Sirius) | Colocates inference and training on the same GPU with millisecond-scale memory handover; the core mechanism is intra-GPU spatial sharing and the paper used 16 GB V100s, so 24 GB is ample. Concern: some experiments span 4 GPUs and the memory-reclaim hooks are tied to a specific PyTorch/CUDA version |
| `eurosys23-with-great-freedom-comes-great-opportuni` | With Great Freedom Comes Great Opportunity: Rethinking Resource Allocation for Serverless Functions | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567506) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567506) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7143413) | Resource-allocation policy for serverless functions - the released artifact bundles the serverless applications, the optimization algorithms, analysis notebooks and the full measured performance dataset, so the policy search can be re-run and improved offline on one machine. Concern - collecting fresh measurements would need a cloud FaaS provider; only the trace-driven half is reproducible locally. Repo link is the Zenodo record - no public GitHub mirror was found. |
| `eurosys24-blox-a-modular-toolkit-for-deep-learning` | Blox: A Modular Toolkit for Deep Learning Schedulers | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629583) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.12621) | [repo](https://github.com/msr-fiddle/blox) | Explicitly a simulator-plus-toolkit for DL cluster scheduling policies, validated by reproducing prior schedulers from traces. Cluster behaviour is simulated, so no multi-node hardware is needed and new policies are cheap to prototype - a near-ideal 10-week improvement target |
| `eurosys24-on-the-limitations-of-carbon-aware-tempo` | On the Limitations of Carbon-Aware Temporal and Spatial Workload Shifting in the Cloud | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650079) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.06502) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10790855) | Trace/simulation study of carbon-aware batch and interactive workload scheduling across 123 regions; the released artifact is a simulator plus carbon-intensity data, so it runs on CPU alone. Concern - it is a limitations study rather than a system, so it suits the "improve the policy / add nuance" angle more than a pure speedup project |
| `eurosys24-orion-interference-aware-fine-grained-gp` | Orion: Interference-aware Fine-grained GPU Sharing for ML Applications | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629578) | [pdf](https://anakli.inf.ethz.ch/papers/orion_eurosys24.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/eth-easl/orion) | Intercepts CUDA kernel launches in user space and schedules operators from co-located clients on one GPU. Single-GPU by design and the whole point is contention on one device. Concern - built against a specific PyTorch/CUDA pairing, so pinning a working toolchain on driver 535 / CUDA 11.8 is the main build risk |
| `eurosys25-a-house-united-within-itself-slo-awarene` | A House United Within Itself: SLO-Awareness for On-Premises Containerized ML Inference Clusters via Faro | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3696071) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.19488) | [repo](https://github.com/beomyeol/faro) | SLO-aware cross-job resource allocation for ML inference driven by production traces; the optimizer and predictor are user-space Python. Concern - the reference stack is Ray Serve on Kubernetes and there is no root or Docker here, so the k8s layer must be replaced by a local Ray cluster or the paper's trace-driven simulator |
| `eurosys25-capsys-contention-aware-task-placement-f` | CAPSys: Contention-aware task placement for data stream processing | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3696085) | — | [repo](https://zenodo.org/doi/10.5281/zenodo.13717642) | Contention-aware operator placement plus interaction with the DS2 autoscaler for Flink; AE Available plus Functional plus Reproduced. Concern - the original setup is a multi-node Kubernetes Flink cluster, so on one host the placement problem can only be emulated with several TaskManagers and cgroup v2 CPU limits, which weakens the multi-node story |
| `eurosys25-dpack-efficiency-oriented-privacy-budget` | DPack: Efficiency-Oriented Privacy Budget Scheduling | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3696096) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2212.13228) | [repo](https://github.com/columbia/dpack) | Privacy budget scheduler formulated as a knapsack problem and evaluated purely in simulation on a synthetic workload derived from an Alibaba cluster trace; CPU only. AE Available plus Functional. Concern - none significant, which also means gains must come from better algorithms rather than systems engineering |
| `eurosys25-eva-cost-efficient-cloud-based-cluster-s` | Eva: Cost-Efficient Cloud-Based Cluster Scheduling | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3717483) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.07437) | [repo](https://github.com/uw-mad-dash/Eva) | Cost-aware cloud cluster scheduler shipped with a large-scale trace-driven simulator alongside the AWS deployment; the simulator runs on a single machine. AE Available plus Functional. Concern - real-cloud validation is out of reach without an AWS budget, so any improvement has to be argued in simulation and with the paper's own cost model |
| `eurosys26-bridging-the-gpu-utilization-gap-predict` | Bridging the GPU Utilization Gap: Predictive Multi-Dimensional Resource Scheduling for AI Workloads | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3803579) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3803579) | — | Predictive multi-dimensional GPU resource scheduler - schedulers of this kind are normally evaluated with cluster traces and a simulator which fits here. Concerns: multi-tenant cluster target and no code found |
| `eurosys26-charm-chiplet-heterogeneity-aware-runtim` | CHARM: Chiplet Heterogeneity-Aware Runtime Mapping System | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3769390) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3769390) | — | User-space runtime that maps tasks to chiplets - this host is a chiplet-based Zen3 Threadripper so the hardware target matches. Concerns: the technique very likely needs PMU-based cache and bandwidth monitoring which is blocked by perf_event_paranoid=3, and no code was found |
| `eurosys26-drops-managing-serverless-resource-pools` | DROPS: Managing Serverless Resource Pools in Microsoft Azure Functions | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3769350) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3769350) | [repo](https://github.com/UWASL/DROPS) | Pre-warmed container pool sizing policies for Azure Functions driven by production allocation traces - trace-driven policy work explicitly in scope. Concern: check whether the artifact is a simulator or needs the real platform |
| `eurosys26-in-production-characterization-of-an-ope` | In-Production Characterization of an Open Source Serverless Platform and New Scaling Strategies | EUROSYS 2026 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3769377) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3767295.3769377) | [repo](https://github.com/ubc-cirrus-lab/femux) | Serverless scaling strategies evaluated against released production traces - trace-driven policy work explicitly in scope, artifact and traces are public |
| `mlsys25-rubick-exploiting-job-reconfigurability` | Rubick: Exploiting Job Reconfigurability for Deep Learning Cluster Scheduling | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/270339c997293ca2988c62f4308e389f-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/270339c997293ca2988c62f4308e389f-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/AlibabaPAI/reconfigurable-dl-scheduler) | DL cluster scheduler exploiting job reconfigurability - artifact ships a cluster simulator driven by pre-measured throughput tables so the scheduling policy can be studied without the 64-GPU cluster - concerns: repo is named Morphling, AE path assumes Docker but conda setup instructions are also provided |
| `mlsys25-venn-resource-management-for-collaborati` | Venn: Resource Management For Collaborative Learning Jobs | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/7fd522b89ac21009b7bbe7560a9a5add-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/7fd522b89ac21009b7bbe7560a9a5add-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SymbioticLab/Venn) | Resource manager for collaborative-learning jobs - conda-based simulator over device traces, no cluster needed - repo name Venn matches the paper even though the system is called Auxo in the text |
| `mlsys26-earthsight-a-distributed-framework-for-l` | EarthSight: A Distributed Framework for Low-Latency Satellite Intelligence | MLSYS 2026 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/26289c647c6828e862e271ca3c490486-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2026/file/26289c647c6828e862e271ca3c490486-Paper-Conference.pdf) | — | Ground-station query scheduler plus dynamic filter ordering, evaluated with a Python constellation simulator, so the scheduling contribution is trace/simulation driven and fits one machine. | `nsdi23-shockwave-fair-and-efficient-cluster-sch` | Shockwave: Fair and Efficient Cluster Scheduling for Dynamic Adaptation in Machine Learning | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/zheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-zheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/uw-mad-dash/shockwave) | ML cluster scheduler whose main evaluation arm is a trace-driven discrete-event simulator that runs entirely on one CPU machine - Concern: the physical-cluster half of the paper used dozens of GPUs so only the simulation results are reproducible or improvable here |

| `nsdi24-characterization-of-large-language-model` | Characterization of Large Language Model Development in the Datacenter | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/hu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-hu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/InternLM/AcmeTrace) | Six-month LLM development trace from a 2048-GPU datacenter is openly released (109 MB on GitHub, ~80 GB full set on Hugging Face) and supports trace-driven scheduling and failure-recovery simulation on one machine. Concerns: this is a characterization paper - the released artifact is the trace plus analysis code, not the described systems (fault-tolerant pretraining, decoupled scheduling), so an improvement project would have to be simulation-based. |
| `nsdi24-solving-max-min-fair-resource-allocation` | Solving Max-Min Fair Resource Allocations Quickly on Large Graphs | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/namyar-solving) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-namyar-solving.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/Soroush) | Fast max-min fair allocators evaluated on WAN traffic engineering and cluster scheduling workloads (public Philly traces); Python plus C# with Gurobi, CPU only, trace-driven. Concern: paper evaluates on up to 140 cores across a cluster; on 16C/32T the microservice graphs (DeathStarBench-style) must be scaled down, and load generators compete with the services for cores. |
| `osdi23-cilantro-performance-aware-resource-allo` | Cilantro: Performance-Aware Resource Allocation for General Objectives via Online Feedback | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/bhardwaj) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-bhardwaj.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/romilbhardwaj/cilantro) | The contribution is an online-learning resource-allocation policy with confidence bounds - pure Python, simulatable from traces. Concern: every released experiment targets a Kubernetes/EKS cluster and ships a Dockerfile, and this machine has no root and no Docker, so the policies would have to be driven by a locally written trace-driven simulator or by cgroup-v2-limited processes. |
| `osdi23-karma-resource-allocation-for-dynamic-de` | Karma: Resource Allocation for Dynamic Demands | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/vuppalapati) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-vuppalapati.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/resource-disaggregation/karma) | A credit-based generalisation of max-min fairness evaluated with trace-driven simulation over production workloads - precisely the kind of policy work scope.md allows to run on one machine. | `osdi24-when-will-my-ml-job-finish-toward-provid` | When will my ML Job finish? | `osdi25-decouple-and-decompose-scaling-resource` | Decouple and Decompose: Scaling Resource Allocation with DeDe | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/xu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-xu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/illinois-nsai/dede) | Released as a plain Python package; the three case studies (cluster scheduling, traffic engineering, load balancing) are solved offline from traces on CPU only - precisely the "schedulers studied by simulation or traces" case in scope. Concern: baselines rely on a commercial solver (Gurobi academic license needed, user-space install); 16 cores limits the parallel-subproblem scaling claims. |
| `osdi25-kamino-efficient-vm-allocation-at-scale` | Kamino: Efficient VM Allocation at Scale with Latency-Driven Cache-Aware Scheduling | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/domingo) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-domingo.pdf) | — | Cache-aware request-scheduling algorithm evaluated with a high-fidelity simulator on production traces - simulation-only, so it fits the machine. Concern on code: the artifact carries Available + Functional + Reproduced badges on the OSDI '25 AE results page, but the PDF contains no repository URL, the authors' project page (daviddomingo.com/projects/Kamino) says "more information to be released", and GitHub search found nothing - repo marked unknown rather than out-no-code. Second concern: the Azure production traces are likely not public. |

Concerns: the paper's generality claim spans ten XPUs - only one NVIDIA Ampere GPU is available here, and the highest hardware-preemption level may require capabilities the A5000 or driver 535 does not expose. |
| `osdi26-nixie-efficient-transparent-temporal-mul` | Nixie: Efficient, Transparent Temporal Multiplexing for Consumer GPUs | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/xu-yechen) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-xu-yechen.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/XOR-op/Nixie) | Best-matched paper in the venue for this machine: a *user-space system service* that time-multiplexes several near-GPU-memory-filling ML jobs on a **consumer GPU** with "no application or driver changes", plus an MLFQ-style scheduler. Eval repo: https://github.com/XOR-op/nixie-eval . Concern: pinned-host-memory pressure experiments assume a large host RAM budget (we have it) |
| `osdi26-spade-signal-aware-dag-scheduling-and-dy` | SPADE: Signal-Aware DAG Scheduling and Dynamic Provisioning for Data Processing Clusters | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/lechowicz) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-lechowicz.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/umass-solar/adaptive-dag) | Explicitly in-scope form: besides the Spark-on-Kubernetes prototype the authors release a *high-fidelity Spark simulator* driven by Alibaba/TPC-H traces plus carbon/price signals, which runs fine on one CPU-only host. Merge note: the scout's repo-link cell held the PDF URL; the repository given in the scout's prose (https://github.com/umass-solar/adaptive-dag) is used here. |
| `sigmetrics24-bones-near-optimal-neural-enhanced-video` | BONES: Near-Optimal Neural-Enhanced Video Streaming | SIGMETRICS 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3656014) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.09920) | [repo](https://github.com/UMass-LIDS/bones) | A Lyapunov-based controller that jointly schedules a GPU super-resolution enhancement queue against network downloads - a GPU resource manager studied in simulation with real network traces, with code explicitly released in the abstract. Concern: the reference prototype is a Kubernetes autoscaler evaluated on a commercial cloud, and this machine has no root, no Docker and no second node, so the k8s prototype path is closed and the project would have to live in the simulator or in a local process-level re-implementation. |

Concern: the core contribution is a competitive-ratio proof for double-threshold algorithms, so the artifact is a numerical simulator rather than a system; an "improvement" here would be algorithmic or an empirical extension (e.g. driving it with real checkpoint/restore costs measured on this machine) rather than a systems build. |
| `sigmetrics25-improving-multiresource-job-scheduling-w` | Improving Multiresource Job Scheduling with Markovian Service Rate Policies | SIGMETRICS 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3727117) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.08915) | [repo](https://github.com/jcpwfloi/msr-borg) | Multiresource job scheduling policy class evaluated by trace-driven simulation on the Google Borg trace; C++ simulator, user space, no cluster needed. | `nsdi24-harvesting-idle-memory-for-application-m` | Harvesting Idle Memory for Application-managed Soft State with Midas | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/qiao) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-qiao.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/uclasystem/midas) | Soft-memory abstraction that lets applications harvest idle memory for caches and memoization; released as a C/C++ user-space library plus a coordinator daemon, and a 125 GiB RAM machine suits the workload. Concern (important): the paper states the coordinator is a user-space daemon plus a privileged kernel module, and the repo contains a koord kernel module - loading it needs root, so cross-application memory rebalancing may not be reproducible and only single-application soft-memory experiments may work. |
| `osdi23-tailcheck-a-lightweight-heap-overflow-de` | TAILCHECK: A Lightweight Heap Overflow Detection Mechanism with Page Protection and Tagged Pointers | OSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi23/presentation/gopal) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi23-gopal.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SBULeeLab/TailCheck) | Implemented as LLVM compiler passes plus an extended mimalloc allocator; relies only on ordinary x86-64 pointer-tag bits and mmap page protection, all reachable without root. LLVM and the SPEC-style benchmarks build in user space. Concerns: the overhead numbers were measured with hardware the report does not fully specify, and the many PROT_NONE guard mappings may bump into the default vm.max_map_count which cannot be raised without root. |
| `osdi26-obase-object-based-address-space-enginee` | OBASE: Object-Based Address-Space Engineering to Improve Memory Tiering | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/banakar) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-banakar.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/WiscADSL/obase) | Compiler + user-space runtime that reorganises the virtual address space so pages become uniformly hot or cold - user-space memory management, no kernel changes. Concern: the paper's testbed has a real slow tier (Optane PMEM); this machine is one NUMA node with no PMEM/CXL, so a second tier would have to be emulated (cgroup limits + NVMe swap/zswap), which changes the measurement |
| `osdi26-osprey-transparent-and-efficient-virtual` | Osprey: Transparent and Efficient Virtual Memory for Secure Computation | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/liu-yicheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-liu-yicheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/mpc-systems/osprey-project) | A user-space, runtime virtual-memory layer that pages secure-computation working sets to SSD transparently - user-space memory management plus NVMe, no trusted hardware needed (this is MPC, not TEE). Concern: MPC parties are usually processes on separate hosts; they would have to be co-located as local processes |
| `osdi26-shaving-the-peaks-taming-tail-latency-fo` | Shaving the Peaks: Taming Tail Latency for Managed Workloads via Disaggregated Garbage Collection | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/lyu) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-lyu.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/SJTU-IPADS/dgc-artifacts) | Moves GC work off the mutator's CPU budget to cut tail latency in multi-tenant JVM workloads; single-server, user-space JVM/runtime work, and cgroup v2 cpu/memory controllers are delegated here. Concerns: artifact asks for a dual-socket NUMA Intel server with hyper-threading **disabled** (needs root) and assumes 32 physical cores per socket; this machine is single-socket, 16 cores |
| `sigmod23-one-shot-garbage-collection-for-in-memor` | One-shot garbage collection for in-memory OLTP through temporality-aware version storage | SIGMOD 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3588699) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3588699) | — | One-shot GC and temporality-aware version storage for in-memory MVCC OLTP - user-space memory reclamation on one multicore machine. Concern: no public repo located via GitHub search or web search; marked unknown rather than excluded |
| `sigmod23-pea-hash-a-performant-extendible-adaptiv` | Pea Hash: A Performant Extendible Adaptive Hashing Index | SIGMOD 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3588962) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3588962) | [repo](https://github.com/schencoding/peahash) | Extendible adaptive hash index trading latency against memory utilization; repo ships a DRAM-only variant. Concern: best results assume Optane PMEM and AVX-512 - the Zen3 Threadripper has neither, so expect a scaled-down baseline |

Concern: the second half of the paper (exmap) is a Linux kernel module and cannot be built or loaded without root; only vmcache itself is reproducible here |
| `sigmod26-analysis-and-evaluation-of-using-microse` | Analysis and Evaluation of Using Microsecond-Latency Memory for In-Memory Indices and Caches in SSD-Based Key-Value Stores | SIGMOD 2026 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3769759) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3769759) | — | analysis of microsecond-latency secondary memory for in-memory indices and caches of SSD-based KV stores with user-level prefetching - memory-tiering study done in user space - concern: original setup emulates a special memory device |
| `sigmod26-predictive-translation-high-performance` | Predictive Translation: High-Performance Buffer Management Without the Trade-Offs | SIGMOD 2026 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3786678) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3786678) | — | buffer manager that replaces pointer chasing with deterministic page placement, explicitly avoiding kernel modules - user-space memory management for larger-than-memory data - concern: no repo located |
| `sigmod26-translytical-processing-via-db-os-co-des` | Translytical Processing via DB-OS Co-designed Buffer: Cross-Engine Isolation and Tunable Update Visibility for HTAP | SIGMOD 2026 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3802123) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3802123) | — | DB-OS co-designed buffer virtualization using huge-page-backed segments remapped read-only between PostgreSQL and DuckDB - implemented as a user-level library - concern: explicit huge pages normally need root, only transparent huge pages are available here - no repo located |
| `socc24-memory-management-in-complex-join-querie` | Memory Management in Complex Join Queries: A Re-evaluation Study | SOCC 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3698038.3698565) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3698038.3698565) | [repo](https://github.com/shivajah/Memory-Management-in-Complex-Join-Queries-A-Re-evaluation-Study) | Re-evaluates 1990s memory-distribution policies across join operators using a modified AsterixDB - user-space memory budgeting, single node, JVM only (Java installable via conda). Directly matches the course's fallback "careful reproduction" track and leaves obvious room for an improved memory-distribution policy. Concerns - original runs were on AWS HDD/SSD instances, so device-level results will differ; it is a study paper, so the "improvement" angle has to be supplied by the team. |
| `sosp25-scalable-far-memory-balancing-faults-and` | Scalable Far Memory: Balancing Faults and Evictions | SOSP 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3731569.3764842) | [pdf](https://infoscience.epfl.ch/handle/20.500.14299/254799) | [repo](https://github.com/rs3lab/mage-artifact) | Scope explicitly allows far-memory studied in user space, and this paper implements its three principles in both the Linux kernel *and* a library OS, so the library-OS (user-space) path is the reproducible one. Concerns - the kernel-side half needs root and a custom kernel and is out of reach; the far-memory backend normally needs a second machine or RDMA and would have to be emulated locally; the scalability claims target far higher core counts than 32 threads, so the interesting high-thread regime is partly out of reach. |

| `vldb24-splitdf-splitting-dataframes-for-memory` | SplitDF: Splitting Dataframes for Memory-Efficient Data Analysis | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3665844.3665849) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol17/p2175-kakaraparthy.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/UWQuickstep/splitting) | memory-footprint reduction for dataframes on DuckDB/Ibis; user-space Python |
| `vldb25-graphcsr-a-degree-equalized-csr-format-f` | GraphCSR: A Degree-Equalized CSR Format for Large-scale Graph Processing | VLDB 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3749646.3749691) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol18/p4255-gan.pdf) | [repo](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/GraphCSR-450E) | Degree-equalized CSR format that cuts memory footprint and improves batch access; single-node evaluation is meaningful. Concerns: the headline result uses a 79024-node supercomputer and the only artifact link is an anonymous.4open.science repo |
| `vldb25-saving-private-hash-join` | Saving Private Hash Join | VLDB 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3742728.3742762) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol18/p2748-kuiper.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/lnkuiper/experiments) | Larger-than-memory hash join with a unified buffer pool and memory allocation across concurrent joins, implemented in DuckDB; single node |

Open-sourced in the paper. Replicas are user-space Java processes, so a 4-16 replica deployment fits in 32 threads with emulated latency. Concerns: the README expects Docker for the Batfish verification service (Docker is not installed - Batfish would have to be built from source or replaced) and an OpenAI API key, which costs money; a local 7-8B model on the A5000 is a plausible but unvalidated substitute. |

Active repo (189 stars). Concerns - requires a paid LLM API for checker synthesis, which is a real budget constraint; and the systems tie-in is analysis tooling rather than a runtime, so "performance" means synthesis cost / false-positive rate. |

| `vldb24-can-large-language-models-predict-data-c` | Can Large Language Models Predict Data Correlations from Column Names? | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3625054.3625066) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol16/p4310-trummer.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/itrummer/DataCorrelationPredictionWithNLP) | A learned component for database tuning - predicting column correlations from schema text to prioritize data profiling and correct cardinality estimates, benchmarked against classical correlation-detection algorithms. Artifact plus benchmark are released. Machine fit is easy: the paper fine-tunes DistilBERT/ALBERT/RoBERTa on a single V100 (p3.2xlarge), all of which fit in 24 GB, and a modern 7-8B instruct model in FP16 also fits on the A5000 for an "improvement" angle. Concerns: this is a study rather than a system, so the improvement must be framed as a better predictor plus a demonstrated downstream benefit (e.g. feeding an optimizer), otherwise it degenerates into a pure reproduction - acceptable as the fallback track but worth flagging. [PVLDB 16(13), added from the vldbgap-2024 shard] |
| `vldb24-conttune-continuous-tuning-by-conservati` | ContTune: Continuous Tuning by Conservative Bayesian Optimization for Distributed Stream Data Processing Systems | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3625054.3625064) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol16/p4282-shao.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ljqcodelove/ContTune) | ML-based configuration tuning - conservative Bayesian Optimization plus a Big-small decomposition that picks per-operator parallelism for Flink jobs, compared against DS2. The tuner itself is user-space Java and the search-quality hypothesis (fewer reconfigurations, no SLA violation) can be tested against the same baselines. Concern: the paper's setup is Flink 1.13 with 45 TaskManagers across three 48-core machines (max parallelism 90); our 16-core/32-thread node cannot reproduce that scale. Flink runs fine as a local multi-process cluster, so the experiment must be scaled down (fewer slots, smaller source rates) or driven by the paper's workload replay - this is the main risk and is flagged for the audit stage rather than being a hard blocker. [PVLDB 16(13), added from the vldbgap-2024 shard] |

Concern: the subject matter is chiplet interconnect architecture rather than systems software, so topic fit needs an audit decision |
| `atc24-kivi-verification-for-cluster-management` | Kivi: Verification for Cluster Management | ATC 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc24/presentation/liu-bingzhe) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc24-liu-bingzhe.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/bingzheliu/Kivi) | Model checker for Kubernetes controllers and configurations; the artifact is a user-space Python plus model-checking toolchain and the state-space reduction is a clean performance target. | `atc25-irhash-efficient-multi-language-compiler` | IRHash: Efficient Multi-Language Compiler Caching by IR-Level Hashing | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/landsberg) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-landsberg.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/luhsra/IRHash) | Multi-language compilation cache keyed on LLVM IR hashes; CPU-only build-system experiments, and clang/LLVM is installable per-user through conda. | `atc25-liteshield-secure-containers-via-lightwe` | LITESHIELD: Secure Containers via Lightweight, Composable Userspace μKernel Services | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/manakkal) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-manakkal.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/kmanakk1/liteshield) | Secure containers built from composable user-space microkernel services serving most Linux syscalls in user space; the LiteShield side needs no root. Concern: baselines (Kata, KVM) need virtualization that is unavailable, so comparisons would shrink to gVisor-class user-space baselines |
| `atc25-pathweaver-a-high-throughput-multi-gpu-s` | PathWeaver: A High-Throughput Multi-GPU System for Graph-Based Approximate Nearest Neighbor Search | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/kim) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-kim.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/AIS-SNU/PathWeaver) | Multi-GPU graph-based ANNS; section 5.3 explicitly reports single-GPU gains for ghost staging and the direction-guided techniques, so there is a meaningful single-GPU scale-down. Concern: pipelining-based path extension is inherently multi-GPU and billion-scale datasets exceed 24 GB |
| `atc25-revealing-floating-point-accumulation-or` | Revealing Floating-Point Accumulation Orders in Software/Hardware Implementations | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/xie) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-xie.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/peichenxie/FPRev) | User-space testing tool that reveals floating-point accumulation orders in BLAS/GPU implementations; runs entirely in user space on CPU and one GPU. | `atc25-the-koala-benchmarks-for-the-shell-chara` | The Koala Benchmarks for the Shell: Characterization and Implications | ATC 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/atc25/presentation/lamprou) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/atc25-lamprou.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/kbensh/koala) | Shell benchmark suite plus automation and characterization infrastructure; pure user-space CPU workloads, no root needed. Concern: it is a benchmark artifact, so an improvement project would target shell runtimes measured with it |
| `eurosys23-effective-performance-issue-diagnosis-wi` | Effective Performance Issue Diagnosis with Value-Assisted Cost Profiling | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3587444) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3587444) | [repo](https://github.com/wenglingmei/vprofAE) | vProf is a user-space profiler built on Clang/LLVM instrumentation plus a modified gprof SIGPROF sampler - notably it does not need hardware performance counters, which are blocked on this machine. Artifact on Zenodo plus a public GitHub mirror. Concern - reproducing the 15 real-world bugs means building four large applications from source. |
| `eurosys23-groundhog-efficient-request-isolation-in` | Groundhog: Efficient Request Isolation in FaaS | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567503) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567503) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7703209) | In-memory process snapshot/restore between FaaS invocations built entirely in user space on fork plus Linux soft-dirty page tracking - the authors state it needs no changes to the OS kernel, language runtimes or functions. Concern - the end-to-end evaluation drives OpenWhisk with Docker containers, which is not installed and cannot be installed without root; the snapshot/restore mechanism itself can still be benchmarked standalone. Repo link is the Zenodo artifact record - no public GitHub mirror was found. |
| `eurosys23-model-checking-guided-testing-for-distri` | Model Checking Guided Testing for Distributed Systems | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3587442) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3587442) | [repo](https://github.com/Icysandwich/Mocket) | Mocket drives a TLA+ model checker to guide implementation-level testing of Java distributed systems (ZooKeeper and similar); everything runs as ordinary local JVM processes, no cluster needed. Concern - Java must be installed in user space, and state-space exploration time is the main cost, which is itself a plausible improvement target. |
| `eurosys23-omni-paxos-breaking-the-barriers-of-part` | Omni-Paxos: Breaking the Barriers of Partial Connectivity | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3587441) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3587441) | [repo](https://github.com/haraldng/omnipaxos-artifacts) | Borderline, kept per the borderline rule. The artifact is a user-space Rust library whose core is deliberately I/O-free and network-agnostic, so a replica group can be run as several processes (or in-process) on one host and partitions can be injected at the transport layer without root. Concern - every headline result in the paper (partial network partitions, reconfiguration I/O at the leader) was measured on a real multi-node cluster, which this machine cannot provide; a project would have to re-frame the claims as simulation. |
| `eurosys23-viper-a-fast-snapshot-isolation-checker` | Viper: A Fast Snapshot Isolation Checker | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567492) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567492) | [repo](https://github.com/Khoury-srg/Viper) | Offline black-box checker over recorded transaction histories - a single-machine C++/SAT-solver pipeline with a companion ViperBench workload repo (figshare artifact also released). | `eurosys23-waffle-exposing-memory-ordering-bugs-eff` | WAFFLE: Exposing Memory Ordering Bugs Efficiently with Active Delay Injection | EUROSYS 2023 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3552326.3567507) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3552326.3567507) | [repo](https://github.com/bastoica/waffle) | Active delay injection for concurrency bug detection - user-space instrumentation with no kernel, root or perf dependency. | `eurosys24-efficient-auditing-of-event-driven-web-a` | Efficient Auditing of Event-driven Web Applications | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650089) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3650089) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10790870) | Karousos is a record-replay runtime for Node.js web applications; the improvement axis is the recording/replay overhead, which is a straightforward user-space runtime performance problem on a single host. Concern - framed as a security-auditing paper rather than a classic performance paper, so the hypothesis has to be written carefully around overhead |
| `eurosys24-lsgraph-a-locality-centric-high-performa` | LSGraph: A Locality-centric High-performance Streaming Graph Engine | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3650076) | [pdf](https://theqihao.github.io/files/LSGraph.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/CGCL-codes/LSGraph) | Shared-memory streaming-graph engine (hierarchical indexed representation + locality-aware update). Single-machine multicore C++, competitors Terrace/Aspen/PaC-tree are also open source, so head-to-head comparison is cheap. Concern - the largest graphs used are multi-hundred-GB on disk; smaller SNAP graphs are a fine scale-down |
| `eurosys24-traceupscaler-upscaling-traces-to-evalua` | TraceUpscaler: Upscaling Traces to Evaluate Systems at High Load | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629581) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3629581) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10042017) | The core contribution is a trace-transformation algorithm (decouple arrival timestamps from request payloads) - pure user-space trace processing, methodology directly reusable by other trace-driven projects. Concern - the published validation replays traces against real servers; on one host this becomes a loopback/local-process experiment at reduced load |
| `eurosys24-unison-a-parallel-efficient-and-user-tra` | Unison: A Parallel-Efficient and User-Transparent Network Simulation Kernel | EUROSYS 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3627703.3629574) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3627703.3629574) | [repo](https://github.com/NASA-NJU/UNISON-for-ns-3) | Parallel discrete-event simulation kernel for ns-3 - entirely user-space CPU work, headline result is a 40x speedup on 24 cores, which maps well onto 16 cores / 32 threads. | `eurosys25-alloystack-a-library-operating-system-fo` | AlloyStack: A Library Operating System for Serverless Workflow Applications | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3717490) | — | [repo](https://github.com/tanksys/AlloyStack) | User-space library OS that shares one address space across serverless workflow functions and isolates them with MPK; the target CPU reports the pku and ospke flags so unprivileged pkey_alloc works. | `eurosys25-collaborative-text-editing-with-eg-walke` | Collaborative Text Editing with Eg-walker: Better, Faster, Smaller | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3696076) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.14252) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.13823409) | Collaborative editing algorithm in Rust with a single-process benchmark harness measuring CPU time and memory only - zero hardware dependencies. | `eurosys25-empowering-webassembly-with-thin-kernel` | Empowering WebAssembly with Thin Kernel Interfaces | EUROSYS 2025 | [page](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689031.3717470) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.03858) | [repo](https://github.com/arjunr2/WALI) | WALI extends a user-space Wasm engine to expose Linux syscalls directly - a pure user-space runtime contribution buildable in $HOME. Concern: several of the six plugins validate findings by running xfstests and e2fsprogs against mounted ext4/XFS instances, which needs root; those plugins could not be reproduced, so a project here would have to target the static-analysis side (e.g. extending to more file systems, improving false-positive rate) rather than the mount-based validation. |
| `fast25-oasis-an-out-of-core-approximate-graph-s` | Oasis: An Out-of-core Approximate Graph System via All-Distances Sketches | FAST 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast25/presentation/yang) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast25-yang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/tsunyuyang/Oasis) | Single-machine out-of-core system that trades memory for SSD I/O when building and querying All-Distances Sketches; user-space C++ with an explicit memory-budget knob, which suits a 125 GiB / NVMe box well. Concerns - large graph inputs plus multi-times-graph-size sketches must fit in ~257 GB free, so the biggest datasets in the paper may need to be dropped. |
| `fast26-sharpen-the-spec-cut-the-code-a-case-for` | Sharpen the Spec, Cut the Code: A Case for Generative File System with SYSSPEC | FAST 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/fast26/presentation/liu-qingyuan) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/fast26-liu-qingyuan.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/LLMNativeOS/specfs-ae) | Best Paper + Distinguished Artifact. SPECFS runs in userspace via FUSE (confirmed in the paper and in the artifact appendix, which lists fuse/libfuse-dev as the only system requirement) - /dev/fuse and setuid fusermount3 are available on this machine. Concern: the generation pipeline needs an external LLM API key (Gemini or DeepSeek), i.e. network access and some API spend. |
| `middleware23-dynacut-a-framework-for-dynamic-and-adap` | DynaCut: A Framework for Dynamic and Adaptive Program Customization | MIDDLEWARE 2023 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3590140.3629121) | [pdf](https://xiaoguang.wang/files/middleware23-dynacut.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ssrg-vt/DynaCut) | Binary-level, runtime code debloating - rewrites the image of a live process to disable unused or already-executed (e.g. initialization) code and re-enable it on demand, guided by execution-trace differential analysis. Entirely user-space, single-machine, and evaluated on ordinary server binaries such as Redis and Nginx that fit this box easily. Concerns - the process rewriter is a modified CRIU plus CRIT, and CRIU checkpoint/restore normally needs root or CAP_CHECKPOINT_RESTORE; unprivileged operation would have to go through a user namespace (bwrap is available), so feasibility of the very first checkpoint must be confirmed before committing to this paper |
| `middleware24-on-the-semantic-overlap-of-operators-in` | On the Semantic Overlap of Operators in Stream Processing Engines | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3654790) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.00793) | [repo](https://github.com/vincenzo-gulisano/aggspes) | User-space JVM stream processing engines and purpose-built micro-SPEs; all throughput numbers are single-host. Paper carries an explicit "Artifact Availability" statement pointing at this repo. Concern: baselines are Flink/Storm-class engines that need a JDK installed via conda, and the claim to improve is operator-level throughput rather than a distributed property. |
| `middleware24-smvx-multi-variant-execution-on-selected` | sMVX: Multi-Variant Execution on Selected Code Paths | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3654794) | — | [repo](https://github.com/ssrg-vt/sMVX) | In-process monitor plus custom loader, entirely user space, no kernel module and no root; repo builds libmonitor.so / liblmvx.so with plain make and evaluates on user-space server applications. Concern: isolation of the monitor relies on Intel MPK. The Threadripper PRO 5955WX is Zen 3, which does expose protection keys, but the pku CPU flag must be confirmed before committing; without it the MPK path would have to be stubbed or emulated. |
| `middleware24-zkstream-a-framework-for-trustworthy-str` | zkStream: a Framework for Trustworthy Stream Processing | MIDDLEWARE 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3652892.3700763) | — | [repo](https://github.com/Nokia-Bell-Labs/zkstream) | Official artifact repo from the authors' lab, described as "the artifact accompanying our paper". Rust, CPU-only proving and verification, benchmarked with NEXMark-style streaming workloads on one host. Concern: Rust is not installed and must come from rustup in $HOME; the repo ships a Dockerfile but cargo builds work without Docker; ZKP proving is memory-hungry, though 125 GiB RAM is ample. |
| `middleware25-a-hybrid-runtime-for-function-as-a-servi` | A Hybrid Runtime for Function-as-a-Service at the Edge | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770785) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3721462.3770785) | — | RUNE combines containers and WebAssembly (WasmEdge) in one FaaS runtime and trades cold-start latency against idle-container memory - a user-space runtime/placement-policy question that is a natural single-node improvement target (better hybrid placement policy, measured as p99 latency vs. memory). Concern: repo is unknown - the paper states RUNE is open source but no URL surfaced from GitHub search on the system name, the authors' handles (Adam Hall, Umakishore Ramachandran, Georgia Tech), or web search, and the ACM full text is unreachable from this host. Concern: the container half of the hybrid needs a container runtime that is not installed and requires a rootless port. Both must be settled at audit. |
| `middleware25-adjusted-objects-an-efficient-and-princi` | Adjusted Objects: An Efficient and Principled Approach to Scalable Programming | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3730953) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.19495) | [repo](https://github.com/BoubacarKaneTSP/DEGO) | Pure user-space Java concurrency library (DEGO) of "adjusted" shared objects, exactly the "runtimes / concurrency libraries" case in the topic table. Evaluated on a single server-class multicore with JMH-style microbenchmarks plus a Retwis-like workload; 16c/32t and 125 GiB RAM are adequate. JDK installs in user space via conda. No GPU, no root, no kernel features needed. Concern: original numbers come from a machine with more hardware threads, so absolute scalability curves will differ. |
| `middleware25-clair-obscur-the-light-and-shadow-of-sys` | Clair Obscur: The Light and Shadow of System Call Interposition - From Pitfalls to Solutions with K23 | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770772) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3721462.3770772) | [repo](https://gitlab.com/tudelft-ssl/k23) | x86-64 system call interposition by binary rewriting; target ISA matches the Threadripper host and the artifact is user space. Also ships a systematic critique of zpoline/lazypoline, so a reproduction fallback is natural. Concern: the hybrid design combines binary rewriting with "multiple Linux interfaces" - if one of those is seccomp user notification it may need CAP_SYS_ADMIN or a no_new_privs path, and ptrace scope is governed by a sysctl this user cannot change. Worth confirming at audit. Repo URL taken from the paper text. |
| `middleware25-roadrunner-accelerating-data-delivery-to` | Roadrunner: Accelerating Data Delivery to WebAssembly-Based Serverless Functions | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770777) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.01888) | [repo](https://github.com/polaris-slo-cloud/roadrunner) | Rust sidecar shim doing near-zero-copy, serialization-free transfer between WasmEdge VMs using vmsplice/splice - ordinary unprivileged syscalls, no eBPF, no kernel module, single host. Inter-function latency and throughput microbenchmarks are the headline results and are reproducible locally. Concern: the artifact packages functions as OCI bundles for containerd and the paper positions it inside Knative/OpenFaaS; with no Docker or root, the shim would have to be driven directly or through rootless tooling built in $HOME. |
| `middleware25-tiaccoon-unified-access-control-with-mul` | Tiaccoon: Unified Access Control with Multiple Transports in Container Networks | MIDDLEWARE 2025 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721462.3770783) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3721462.3770783) | [repo](https://github.com/hiroyaonoe/tiaccoon) | Go middleware that hooks socket-API system calls and transparently substitutes a faster transport while keeping one access-control policy - a user-space syscall-interposition artifact, and the UNIX-domain-socket fast path plus the policy engine are testable on one host. Borderline, kept per the "note the concern" rule. Concern: the RDMA transport cannot be exercised at all here, and the container-overlay baselines assume a container runtime that is not installed and cannot be apt-installed; network namespaces via unprivileged user namespaces would have to stand in. Repo is marked "[Experimental]" by the author. |
| `mlsys23-hotline-profiler-automatic-annotation-an` | Hotline Profiler: Automatic Annotation and A Multi-Scale Timeline for Visualizing Time-Use in DNN Training | MLSYS 2023 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/hash/347330dd540c72b2c9b0cc304bcf43c6-Abstract-mlsys2023.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2023/file/347330dd540c72b2c9b0cc304bcf43c6-Paper-mlsys2023.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/UofT-EcoSystem/hotline) | Profiler that annotates standard PyTorch or Chrome trace data with DNN training-loop and model structure - explicitly avoids vendor-specific tools so it needs no perf counters and no root - concern - visualization front end plus a Zenodo artifact so check the trace collector still works with current PyTorch |
| `mlsys25-the-hidden-bloat-in-machine-learning-sys` | The Hidden Bloat in Machine Learning Systems | MLSYS 2025 | [page](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/5321b1dabcd2be188d796c21b733e8c7-Abstract-Conference.html) | [pdf](https://proceedings.mlsys.org/paper_files/paper/2025/file/5321b1dabcd2be188d796c21b733e8c7-Paper-Conference.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/reSecureIt/negativa-ml) | User-space debloating of ML framework shared libraries including GPU fatbins - no root, no kernel, measures file size, memory and runtime - excellent fit for this machine (repo redirects from negativa-ai/negativa-ml) |

Concerns: GPU-side counter collection goes through CUPTI, which NVIDIA restricts to admin users by default, and the paper's value proposition is fleet-scale TPU profiling that cannot be reproduced |

| `nsdi23-fast-approximate-vector-queries-on-very` | Fast, Approximate Vector Queries on Very Large Unstructured Datasets | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/zhang-zili) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-zhang-zili.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/pkusys/Auncel) | Vector query engine that builds a per-query error-latency profile - the single-worker path is the interesting mechanism and runs in user space - Concern: headline numbers use DEEP1B on four c5.metal instances so experiments must be scaled to SIFT or GIST million-to-100M datasets to fit 125 GiB RAM and 257 GB disk |
| `nsdi23-push-button-reliability-testing-for-clou` | Push-Button Reliability Testing for Cloud-Backed Applications with Rainmaker | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/chen-yinfang) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-chen-yinfang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/xlab-uiuc/rainmaker) | User-space REST fault-injection tool for cloud-backed applications needing no root kernel or hardware support - Concern: the .NET toolchain must be installed under HOME and reproducing the reported bugs needs cloud SDK targets or local emulators such as Azurite rather than real cloud accounts |

| `nsdi23-the-benefit-of-hindsight-tracing-edge-ca` | The Benefit of Hindsight: Tracing Edge-Cases in Distributed Systems | NSDI 2023 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi23/presentation/zhang-lei) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi23-zhang-lei.pdf) | [repo](https://gitlab.mpi-sws.org/cld/tracing/hindsight) | Always-on retroactive distributed tracing implemented as a user-space C library plus agent needing no root perf counters or eBPF - Concern: the reference evaluation drives DeathStarBench microservices under Docker across several nodes so it must be re-hosted as multiple processes on one machine - code lives on MPI-SWS GitLab not GitHub |

Concerns: Java toolchain must be installed via conda; running small local clusters is RAM and disk heavy but fits; some fault types used in related work rely on tc (root-only) - FUSE-based faults are available. |

Concerns: needs a Java toolchain via conda and specific versions of the target systems (Hadoop, Solr, Cassandra) to reproduce case studies; taint tracking has high runtime overhead so experiments are CPU-bound but fit in 16 cores. |
| `nsdi24-finding-adversarial-inputs-for-heuristic` | Finding Adversarial Inputs for Heuristics using Multi-level Optimization | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/namyar-finding) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-namyar-finding.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/microsoft/MetaOpt) | Tool that encodes a heuristic and its optimal counterpart for a solver to find worst-case performance gaps; runs entirely in user space on CPU. Concerns: needs Gurobi (free academic license) and the .NET/C# toolchain, neither preinstalled but both installable in $HOME; the contribution is an optimization/encoding framework, so an improvement would be algorithmic rather than systems-performance. |
| `nsdi24-ldb-an-efficient-latency-profiling-tool` | LDB: An Efficient Latency Profiling Tool for Multithreaded Applications | NSDI 2024 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi24/presentation/cho) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-cho.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/inhocho89/llvm14-ldb) | Software-only latency profiler using LLVM instrumentation plus a busy-polling stack-scanning thread; explicitly designed to avoid hardware performance counters and kernel support, which matches a machine with perf_event_paranoid=3 and eBPF disabled. Concerns: requires building a custom LLVM 14 in user space (long build); dedicates one core to the scanner (fine on 32 threads); some applications evaluated in the paper are kernel-bypass runtimes that cannot run here, so plain Linux workloads must be substituted. |

Concerns: the DPDK and eBPF-XDP variants need root and eBPF (blocked), so only the CPU variant is usable; packet traces (for example CAIDA) must be obtained separately and the no-DPDK setup changes the achievable packet rates. |

Repo has no README on main - code is on the master branch. |

Concerns: the repo README is essentially empty, so documentation is thin; the routing-convergence emulation part of the evaluation may need more setup than the data-structure benchmarks. |

Concern: none for the machine; the improvement would be data-structure/algorithm engineering rather than a distributed-systems build. |

Available + Functional + Reproduced. Concerns: "various network conditions" results were obtained across machines - on one host they must be emulated with loopback delay (tc needs root, so an application-level delay injector is required instead). |

Concern: evaluation drives four large JVM distributed systems (HDFS/HBase/Kafka/ZooKeeper) - must be run pseudo-distributed as multiple processes on the one host, and 125 GiB RAM should suffice but overhead numbers may shift. |
| `osdi25-deterministic-client-enforcing-determini` | Deterministic Client: Enforcing Determinism on Untrusted Machine Code | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/yedidia) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-yedidia.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/zyedidia/decl) | Pure user-space SFI-style verifier/rewriter and sandbox runtime for x86-64 machine code, evaluated with SPEC-style CPU benchmarks and a smart-contract engine; no kernel, no hypervisor, no counters needed. Concerns: builds on a patched LLVM (long user-space build); the Arm64 half cannot be evaluated on this x86 host; the paper did not participate in artifact evaluation, but the authors' repo is public and self-described as the research artifact. |
| `osdi25-extending-applications-safely-and-effici` | Extending Applications Safely and Efficiently | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/zheng-yusheng) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-zheng-yusheng.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/eunomia-bpf/bpftime) | bpftime runs eBPF-style extensions entirely in *user space* via dynamic binary rewriting - that is the whole point of the paper, so unprivileged_bpf_disabled=2 does not block it; mature repo, Available + Functional + Reproduced. Concerns: the isolation story uses Intel MPK/PKU, which must be verified on this Zen3 Threadripper; and the kernel-eBPF comparison baselines in the paper need root, so those numbers cannot be re-measured. |
| `osdi25-neutrino-fine-grained-gpu-kernel-profili` | Neutrino: Fine-grained GPU Kernel Profiling via Programmable Probing | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/huang-songlin) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-huang-songlin.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/open-neutrino/neutrino) | Deliberately hardware-counter-free GPU profiler (assembly-layer probes, user-space driver interception), so perf_event_paranoid=3 and the lack of root are not blockers. Available + Functional + Reproduced. Concern: SASS probing is tied to specific driver/CUDA versions - needs checking against driver 535.216.01 and sm_86. |
| `osdi25-paralegal-practical-static-analysis-for` | Paralegal: Practical Static Analysis for Privacy Bugs | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/adam) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-adam.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/brownsys/paralegal) | Rust static analyzer (PDG extraction + policy checking); entirely user-space, CPU-only, with a Zenodo artifact DOI and Available + Functional + Reproduced badges. Concerns: needs a pinned Rust nightly toolchain plus rustc internals (installable via rustup into $HOME); the eight evaluated applications are large Rust codebases whose builds are long but single-machine. |
| `osdi25-quake-adaptive-indexing-for-vector-searc` | Quake: Adaptive Indexing for Vector Search | OSDI 2025 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/mohoney) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi25-mohoney.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/marius-team/quake) | Single-node user-space C++/Python vector index with a latency cost model; baselines (HNSW, DiskANN, SVS, ScaNN) are all user-space libraries. Concern: the paper's NUMA-aware intra-query parallelism cannot be studied on a 1-NUMA-node machine; Wikipedia-scale vector sets must fit the ~257 GB free disk. Artifact Available + Functional, not Reproduced. |
| `osdi26-a-compilation-based-under-constrained-ex` | A Compilation-Based Under-Constrained Execution Engine | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/yin) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-yin.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/R-Fuzz/UCSan) | Compiles individual kernel components into native user-space harnesses and fuzzes them (AFL++) - the *analysis* runs in user space even though the target is kernel code, so no kernel build/boot is needed. Concern: LLVM toolchain must be built per-user; large compile times |
| `osdi26-controlling-opaque-component-effects-wit` | Controlling Opaque-Component Effects with Semisolates and Try | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/lamprou) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-lamprou.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/binpash/try) | The paper explicitly states try is an *unprivileged*, language-agnostic command built on user namespaces / overlay mounts - bwrap-style sandboxing works on this machine. Clean single-machine performance target (effect capture/commit overhead) |
| `osdi26-diagnosing-performance-issues-in-applica` | Diagnosing Performance Issues in Application-Defined Resources | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/hu-yigong) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-hu-yigong.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/BlizzardLab/OmniProfiler) | User-space profiler (gigiprofiler) combining LLM semantic inference with static analysis, evaluated on MariaDB/Redis-class applications on one host. Concern: it "integrates with perf to sample hardware counters" - perf_event_paranoid=3 blocks that path, so only the instrumentation/LLM part is reproducible |
| `osdi26-efficient-gpu-centric-evolving-graph-pro` | Efficient GPU-Centric Evolving Graph Processing at Scale | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/zhang-yunmo) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-zhang-yunmo.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/YunMoZhang/POEGA) | Single-GPU out-of-core graph analytics; the artifact appendix says it was evaluated on an **NVIDIA RTX A4000**, i.e. a smaller Ampere card than ours. Concern: artifact asks for CUDA Toolkit >= 12.4 while the system nvcc is 11.8 and the driver is 535 - a per-user conda CUDA 12.4 toolkit is needed and minor-version compatibility must hold |

| `osdi26-mimesys-generating-realistic-executable` | Mimesys: Generating Realistic Executable Testing Environments from Resource Usage Traces | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/kim-donghyun) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-kim-donghyun.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/ldos-project/mimesys) | Synthesises executable contention workloads from resource-usage traces; artifact runs on plain Linux, the diffusion model trains on one GPU, and cgroup v2 cpu/memory delegation supports the contention experiments. Concern: fidelity claims involve memory-bandwidth contention, which is hard to *measure* here without hardware counters |
| `osdi26-mohabi-disaggregating-and-sandboxing-the` | Mohabi: Disaggregating and Sandboxing the Firefox JavaScript Engine | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/sharma) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-sharma.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/UT-Security/jitsbx-root) | Pure user-space software fault isolation of SpiderMonkey inside Firefox; no root, no kernel, single machine. | `osdi26-rt-regular-types-for-the-streaming-shell` | RT: Regular Types for the Streaming Shell | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/li-zekai) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-li-zekai.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/atlas-brown/rt) | Overlay type system for shell stream programs; artifact appendix gives the GitHub repo, checking is CPU-only and user space, and the evaluation corpus is hundreds of public shell programs. | `osdi26-spain-succinct-proofs-for-numerical-comp` | Spain: Succinct Proofs for Numerical Computations | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/destefano) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-destefano.pdf) | [repo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.20090527) | User-space prover/front-end toolchain with a clear, reproducible performance target (prover time and constraint count for float-heavy computations); most benchmarks need under 5 GB RAM. Concerns: the artifact is Docker-based (must be ported to conda/source) and the largest GPT-2 configuration needs ~270 GB RAM (we have 125 GiB) - stick to the small benchmarks |

Concern: the "improvement" metric is bugs found, which is noisy over a 10-week window - a performance/coverage angle would be safer |
| `osdi26-hs-speculative-script-reordering-at-subp` | hS: Speculative Script Reordering at Subprocess Granularity | OSDI 2026 | [page](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi26/presentation/liargkovas) | [pdf](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi26-liargkovas.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/binpash/hs) | Out-of-order speculative execution of shell scripts with runtime effect detection; user-space, no root, benchmarks are local shell pipelines. | `sigmetrics24-agents-of-autonomy-a-systematic-study-of` | Agents of Autonomy: A Systematic Study of Robotics on Modern Hardware | SIGMETRICS 2024 | [page](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3626774) | [pdf](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3626774) | [repo](https://github.com/cmu-roboarch/rowild) | RoWild is an open-source cross-platform user-space robotics benchmark suite (C++/Python, CPU and CUDA) that the paper profiles end-to-end on everything from embedded CPUs to server GPUs; one 16-core CPU plus one A5000 covers the high-end configuration. Concern: the paper is a characterization study and its own conclusions argue for hardware changes, so an improvement project would have to target the software side (kernel fusion, batching, data structures in the motion-planning and SLAM workloads). Second concern: the published characterization leans on architectural counters that perf_event_paranoid=3 blocks, so a reproduction would be limited to wall-clock timing. |

Concern: the objects of study are Docker images (TensorFlow/PyTorch/NVIDIA containers) and Docker is not installed and needs root here, so reproduction would require daemonless user-space image tooling (skopeo/umoci/bwrap) to pull and unpack the layers - a real porting risk that the audit stage should weigh. |

Single GPU, user space, CUDA code in the repo. Our RTX A5000 is Ampere (CC 8.6) and does have 2nd-gen RT cores plus OptiX support, so the mechanism reproduces. Concerns: the paper's result is largely negative (RT cores lose to CUDA except at high skew), which suits the "reproduction with nuance" fallback; OptiX version may want a newer driver than 535, and large graphs must fit in 24 GB. |

Repo title matches the paper exactly. Concerns: contribution is closer to coding theory / distributed protocols than to the topic table's systems categories, and the motivating deployment is blockchain mempool sync - an audit may judge it out of topic. |

Concerns - the kvm isolation backend is unusable (no /dev/kvm) and the Firecracker baseline needs KVM, so only the mmu process-isolation backend is reproducible; the mmu worker README asks for an exec-mounted /dev/shm which normally needs root and would need a workaround. |

Concerns - it is a testing framework, so an "improvement" hypothesis would be about bug-finding throughput, coverage or oracle precision rather than system performance; the repo is small and lightly documented (2 stars). |

Title suggests a lightweight thread or compute-offload abstraction which could be either user space or kernel. |

| `sosp26-rpcshield-defending-microservices-agains` | RPCShield: Defending Microservices Against Cascading Failures | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | [repo](https://github.com/uber-research/rpcshield) | Public repo describes RPCShield as a static analysis tool that infers fail-close versus fail-open RPC dependency relations - static analysis runs fine in user space with no Docker. Concerns: no abstract is public, and the end-to-end cascading-failure evaluation is on Uber production microservices; open microservice benchmarks normally assume Docker which is not installed here. |
| `sosp26-skill-vm-write-once-run-everywhere-effic` | Skill VM: Write Once, Run Everywhere Efficiently | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03088) | [pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.03088) | [repo](https://github.com/SJTU-IPADS/SkVM) | SkVM compiles agent skills against model-harness capability profiles and JIT-solidifies them into code at runtime. Official SJTU-IPADS repo plus a project homepage. Pure user-space runtime. Concern: the evaluation spans eight LLMs and three harnesses, so reproduction is bounded by API cost rather than by hardware. |
| `sosp26-state-aware-fuzzing-of-javascript-engine` | State-Aware Fuzzing of JavaScript Engines with LLM-Guided Instrumentation | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | State-aware JavaScript-engine fuzzing with LLM-guided instrumentation. Engine fuzzing is user space and CPU bound - 16 cores and 32 threads make fuzzing campaigns feasible, and coverage or bugs-per-hour is a clean testable hypothesis. Concerns: this is a testing and security tool rather than a resource-management system, no artifact public yet, and the LLM guidance costs API calls. |
| `sosp26-tamias-feedback-guided-systematic-concur` | Tamias: Feedback-Guided Systematic Concurrency Exploration for Transparent Distributed System Fuzzing | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | Feedback-guided systematic concurrency exploration for transparent distributed-system fuzzing (Purdue - Fonseca and Yongle Zhang, groups that normally release artifacts). Distributed fuzzing of this kind is usually driven from one host with several processes. Concern: no abstract or artifact public yet. |
| `sosp26-testing-custom-control-planes-without-th` | Testing Custom Control Planes Without the Cluster | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | The premise of the paper is testing Kubernetes-style custom control planes without provisioning a cluster, which is exactly the constraint this machine imposes. UCSC (Kuper and Quinn). Concern: no abstract or artifact is public yet, and Go would need a user-space install. |
| `sosp26-validating-rust-compilers-with-trait-typ` | Validating Rust Compilers with Trait-Type Constraint Graph | SOSP 2026 | [page](https://sigops.org/s/conferences/sosp/2026/accepted.html) | — | — | Validating Rust compilers by constructing trait-type constraint graphs to generate valid but tricky programs. Compiler testing is user space and CPU bound; Rust installs per-user via rustup with no root. Concerns: this is a PL testing tool rather than a resource-management system, and no artifact is public yet. |

| `vldb24-rtindex-exploiting-hardware-accelerated` | RTIndeX: Exploiting Hardware-Accelerated GPU Raytracing for Database Indexing | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3625054.3625063) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol16/p4268-schuhknecht.pdf) | [repo](https://gitlab.rlp.net/juhenneb/rtindex) | Maps database point/range indexing onto NVIDIA OptiX raytracing cores; a pure user-space CUDA/OptiX artifact, no root, no kernel, no cluster. The machine fit is unusually good: the paper's Section 4.10 explicitly re-runs the study on Ampere and Turing RTX GPUs besides the RTX 4090, and our RTX A5000 is Ampere with RT cores and exactly 24 GB VRAM (same capacity as their main 4090 system), so datasets sized in the paper fit. Carries the PVLDB "Results Reproduced" badge. Concerns: (a) the artifact is on GitLab (gitlab.rlp.net), reachable and live, not GitHub; (b) the authors use Nsight Systems/Nsight Compute for some breakdowns - GPU profiling may be blocked by the driver's admin-only profiling setting, so a project should plan on wall-clock timing only; (c) OptiX SDK must be installed per-user, and CUDA 11.8/driver 535 support OptiX 7.x. [PVLDB 16(13), added from the vldbgap-2024 shard] |
| `vldb24-rtscan-efficient-scan-with-ray-tracing-c` | RTScan:  Efficient Scan with Ray Tracing Cores | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3648160.3648183) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol17/p1460-lv.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/AntaresAlice/RTScan) | index scan offloaded to GPU ray-tracing cores; the A5000 is Ampere and has RT cores, OptiX works on driver 535 |
| `vldb24-single-update-sketch-with-variable-count` | Single Update Sketch with Variable Counter Structure | VLDB 2024 | [page](https://doi.org/10.14778/3625054.3625065) | [pdf](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol16/p4296-wang.pdf) | [repo](https://github.com/DimitrisMel/SSVS) | Borderline, kept per the "when in doubt, include" rule. SSVS is a compact counting data structure (variable-width active counters, noise-interval estimator) whose released artifact is a plain user-space Java implementation evaluated by replaying packet traces and measuring per-item processing time and estimation error - no root, no kernel, no special hardware, and it runs in minutes on one core. Improving the accuracy/throughput tradeoff or re-testing on non-network streams is a well-scoped hypothesis. Concerns: (a) it is closer to an algorithms/data-structure paper than a systems paper, so topic fit is the weakest of the six; (b) the design's stated target is switch data planes, and the CAIDA traces used for evaluation require a separate data-access agreement, so a substitute trace may be needed. [PVLDB 16(13), added from the vldbgap-2024 shard] |
